## Verdict: ACCEPT with carries

The schema, the triggers, the change_kind planner, the locking and the write-route refusal order are correct. They match
the packet, and I could not break them; the probes are below. There are two Medium findings. Both are confined to the
demo fixture and the idempotency digest, and neither touches the migration. They are cheap now, but become costly once a
walkthrough database holds published digests. **Fix M1 and M2 as the first commit of phase 2, before anything is
published to a walkthrough or demo database.** Nothing in the migration needs to change before merge. L1 is an optional
hardening that is cheapest to make now, because the migration is not merged yet.

## Checks I ran (results as observed)

  own scratch DB `assetops_s402a_review_test`, using `ASSETOPS_TEST_DATABASE_URL`; the DB name must end in `_test`, see
  `test_s1_bindings_postgres.py:67`. Result: **45 passed** (30 + 15).
- **Downgrade.** I ran alembic `downgrade 0009_finding_store` on the scratch DB.
  - Both tables and both functions were gone, and `ledger_append_only()` (owned by an earlier migration) was correctly
    kept.
  - Re-upgrading to head (`0010_financial_assumptions`) was clean.
- **`tools/check-all.ps1`.** All 9 guards passed: architecture, navigation, agent workflow, registry, delivery
  fixtures, mock-gap fixtures, orphan scan (30 tagged / 12 baselined), scan fixtures and screen ledger.
- **Not run.**

- **Reviewer probes.** I wrote a temporary scratch test file, placed it in `backend/tests` only for the run and then
  deleted it. Nothing was committed. It found:
  - **Lock removed, race test reruns.** I replaced `pg_advisory_xact_lock` with a no-op by monkeypatching `text` in
    the store, then reran `test_concurrent_publications_against_one_prior_admit_exactly_one`. It **failed 5/5**, so the
    race test really exercises contention and is not vacuous.
  - **Column updates on assignment rows.** UPDATEs of `id`, `revision_id` and `recorded_from` are refused by the
    immutability trigger. Reopening a closed `recorded_to` to NULL is refused ("already closed").
  - **Two Site series.** Two Sites each get `revision_no = 1`, with no interference between them.
  - **Re-pricing the scheduled v2 at its exact start (2027-01-01).**
    - As a SUCCESSION: refused, `SUCCESSION_OVERLAP_NOT_PERMITTED`.
    - As an open-ended CORRECTION: refused, `CORRECTION_NOT_BACKDATED_OR_BOUNDED`.
    - A **bounded backdated full-window CORRECTION over an Oct-Dec 2026 window succeeds** (revision 3). So the S4-02b
      need (a backdated full-window correction over a Finding window) is not blocked.
  - **Fixture replay after a byte-level change.** Two cases were tested against a DB that already holds v1-v3:
    - appending a v4 step to the fixture;
    - flipping only the line endings.

    Both re-runs exit 3 at **v1** with `IDEMPOTENCY_KEY_REUSED`; see M1.
  - **Retroactive close.** An UPDATE that closes a current assignment at `recorded_from + 1Âµs`, which is earlier than
    later publications, is **accepted** by the database; see L1.

## Focus areas

1. **Migration 0010.**
   - The EXCLUDE (`0010:103-106`) is character-for-character the packet's shape.
   - The non-empty CHECKs hold on both axes (`:100-101`), and on the revision too (`:72`).
   - The set-once trigger (`:121-139`):
     - compares `to_jsonb(NEW) - 'recorded_to'` with OLD;
     - refuses changing a set `recorded_to`;
     - refuses DELETE.
   - The containment trigger (`:143-172`, BEFORE INSERT) does three things:
     - it checks `@>` against the revision's authored range;
     - it requires `recorded_from >= rev.recorded_at`;
     - it enforces series monotonicity. That rule is `>=`, which permits the same-publication siblings; the store
       separately enforces strict `>` for the new instant (`store:131-137`).
   - The revision table is append-only through `ledger_append_only()`.
   - The `numeric` CHECK `value >= 0 AND value < 'Infinity'` correctly excludes NaN as well (the negative, NaN and
     Infinity tests are parametrized).
   - **Boundary instant.** The closed row is `[r_prev, t)` and the new row is `[t, âˆž)`. They are disjoint because the
     ranges are half-open, and `t > r_prev` is guaranteed, so no empty range is possible.
   - **NULL `valid_to`.** It becomes an unbounded upper end in `tstzrange`, which is correct.
   - The UPDATEs that close rows run before the INSERTs of new rows (`store:159-171`), so the non-deferrable exclusion
     is satisfied mid-transaction.
   - The downgrade is safe; see the checks above.
2. **change_kind.**
   - INITIAL, SUCCESSION and CORRECTION match packet item 2 and R1.
   - **Deviation: SUCCESSION/CORRECTION over uncovered time is refused.** This is consistent with the packet, which
     defines uncovered time as INITIAL, and it blocks nothing S4-02b needs. A CORRECTION that partly covers unpriced
     time is still allowed.
   - **Deviation: an open-ended future CORRECTION is refused.** This is the packet's literal "backdated or bounded".
   - **Unlisted deviation (L2).** A SUCCESSION exactly at the start of an open-ended assignment is refused. This
     matches the residual risk the plan re-review already recorded, and S4-02b does not need it.
3. **Idempotency and concurrency.** All five points hold:
   - The lock is taken first (`store:108-110`), keyed by tenant, Site and key, and it is transaction-scoped.
   - The replay is checked before the prior check, so a retry after success replays rather than raising 409.
   - A different digest returns 409.
   - A stale prior returns 409.
   - `clock_timestamp()` is read after the lock.

   Both race tests are real; see the probe.
4. **Actor authority.**
   - `SYSTEM_PUBLISHER` is a code constant, and the CLI has no actor or author flag.
   - The store refuses a non-SYSTEM principal.
   - The DB CHECK ties HUMAN to `author_id` and to `HUMAN_API`.
   - The route refuses in the order 401, then 422, then 403/404, then a 401 for an admitted request
     (`financial_api.py:61-87`).
   - It does not import `development_access`, and that is tested.
5. **Guard.**
   - The rule-8 writer list and per-table ownership are added symmetrically to the existing Finding rule.
   - The null-root skip (`configuration-persistence.ps1:435`) only drops a domain whose `UserRootVar` is `$null`. Only
     `financials` has one, and `ExtraUserRoots` is still iterated. `Writer = $null` was already handled at `:386`.
   - The change is narrow and does not weaken any other domain.
   - I did not re-run the implementer's non-vacuity probes, because they edit committed files. The regex review agrees
     with them: an UPDATE of any other column, or a comma-joined SET, fails `$assumptionUpdate`.
6. **Tests.** See the checks above. The implementer sharpened the history-rewrite test to move `recorded_to` earlier,
   so it targets the set-once trigger rather than the exclusion. That makes it non-vacuous.
7. **Fixture dates.** See M2 and C3.

## Findings

### Medium

**M1. The idempotency digest includes the whole fixture file's bytes, so a fixture replay breaks on any edit to the
file, including a line-ending change.**

Where:
- `publish_assumption.py:98` puts `fixture_sha256 = sha256(raw file bytes)` into `provenance`;
- `assumptions.py:136` puts `provenance` into `request_sha256`.

Failure scenarios, both observed:
- **Appending a step.** On a DB that already holds v1-v3, appending a v4 step (which S4-02b will need; see C3) makes
  `fixture --file ...` exit 3 at **v1** with `IDEMPOTENCY_KEY_REUSED`.
- **Line endings.** Flipping only LF and CRLF does the same.
  - The repo has `core.autocrlf=true` and no `.gitattributes` entry for this file.
  - The working copy is LF today, but a fresh Windows checkout writes CRLF.
  - So a walkthrough DB published from one checkout refuses a re-run from another.

The handoff's claim that "re-running the fixture replays every step" is true only for a byte-identical file.

The same class of problem exists for `SYSTEM_PUBLISHER.authorization_basis` (`assumptions.py:47-51`, digest `:138`).
Rewording that prose would make every stored publication non-replayable.

Why fix it now: once a walkthrough or demo DB holds digests, changing the digest formula makes every stored publication
conflict on replay. Today no persistent DB has been published (phase 2 does that), so the change is free.

Fix:
- Keep `fixture_sha256` and the authorization text in stored provenance, but exclude both from `request_sha256`.
- Alternatively, digest a canonical JSON of the **step** rather than the file.
- Add a test that replays v1-v3 from a copy of the fixture with a v4 appended and the line endings flipped, and expects
  `replayed=True`.

Optionally pin `*.json text eol=lf` for `backend/conversions`.

**M2. The fixture and one test carry a time bomb: the suite starts failing on 2026-12-12 and the fixture on
2027-01-01.**

Where:
- the fixture's v2 `valid_from` is `2027-01-01` (`demo-diesel-price-publications.json:26`);
- the test sets `soon = now + 20 days` (`test_s4_assumptions_postgres.py:190`).

Failure scenarios:
- **From 2026-12-12.** `soon` is at or after 2027-01-01, so it overlaps only v2's open tail, which strictly contains
  it. The SUCCESSION then **succeeds**, and `test_succession_overlapping_more_than_the_open_ended_assignment_is_409`
  fails with DID NOT RAISE.
- **From 2027-01-01.** Every fixture run on a fresh DB is refused at v2 with `SUCCESSION_NOT_FUTURE`. That breaks the
  restart test, the history-rewrite test and the walkthrough recipe.

This lands inside the expected S4-02b and owner-walkthrough period, which is 60-80 days away.

Fix (before the walkthrough DB is published, because of M1):
- move v2 well into the future, for example `2030-01-01`;
- derive the test's SUCCESSION instant from the v2 date (for example `v2 - 30 days`, asserted to be later than now)
  instead of `now + 20 days`.

### Low

**L1. The set-once trigger allows a retroactive close.** (`0010:121-139`)

`recorded_to` may be set to any instant later than `recorded_from`, including one earlier than later publications in
the series.

Failure scenario: a direct SQL writer closes a current row at `recorded_from + 1Âµs`, which the probe showed is
accepted. That silently changes what earlier recorded cutoffs select, and can leave an unpriced hole at a cutoff.
- Only the guarded store writes these tables, and it always closes at a fresh `clock_timestamp()` later than the
  series' latest `recorded_from`. So this is defence in depth, not a live bug.
- The `foundation_revision` precedent has the same gap.

Fix (optional; cheapest before merge, otherwise it needs a new migration): in the immutability trigger, when OLD
`recorded_to` is NULL and NEW is not, require NEW `recorded_to` > the series' `max(recorded_from)`.

**L2. Unlisted deviation: a SUCCESSION at exactly the start of the open-ended assignment is refused.**
(`assumptions.py:196`, `not only.valid_from < valid_from`)

The packet says SUCCESSION "may only truncate the single open-ended current assignment **containing** `valid_from`".
Half-open `[2027-01-01, âˆž)` contains 2027-01-01. The strict `<` refuses that case, and an open-ended future CORRECTION
is also refused, so re-pricing an already-scheduled future price from its own start has no open-ended form. Only a
bounded CORRECTION with an arbitrary far end can do it.

This is pure plan code, with no schema impact and nothing S4-02b needs. The plan re-review already listed the related
residual risk.

Fix: record it as a listed deviation in the phase 2 handoff, and send it to the Architect with the next batched
questions. Do not change it now.

### Nit

- **Guard granularity.** The rule-8 regexes are per line. A single source line holding two statements, the second of
  them an arbitrary UPDATE, would pass. The DB triggers backstop this, and the same is true of the other writers.
- **Route body.** `financial_api.py:66` reads an unbounded request body before the 422 check. This is acceptable while
  the route is 401 by default, but worth a size cap when a human path exists.

## Carries for phase 2 / S4-02b

- **C1 (phase 2, first commit).** Fix M1 and M2 before running
  `publish_assumption fixture` against any walkthrough DB.
- **C2 (phase 2).** Optionally apply L1 while 0010 is unmerged, and list L2 in the handoff's deviations.
- **C3 (S4-02b).** v3 corrects June 2026, so the demo Findings (Oct/Nov 2026) stay on v1. That is fine for the phase 1
  contract, which asked for a middle-only correction and not a Finding-window one. S4-02b's "full-window backdated
  correction" fixture needs a new step, for example v4, a bounded CORRECTION over the selected Finding's whole window.
  - The probe shows the planner accepts it.
  - It replays safely only after M1 is fixed.
- **C4 (phase 2 reads).** Select at a cutoff `c` with `recorded_from <= c < recorded_to`, and select current rows with
  `recorded_to IS NULL`, both half-open. Keep the write route's default `no_verified_principal` when adding read
  routes with a Finding-like access default.

## Residual risk not named in the packet

- **Overlap rules live in plan code.** Which overlaps a change_kind permits is decided in plan code. The DB guarantees
  only that no overlap ever persists and that time is monotonic. So a planner bug would show as an exclusion error
  (fail-closed) or as a lawful-but-wrong split; the database would not catch the latter. The 30 pure plan tests cover
  the stated cases.
- **No `.gitattributes` for fixtures.** Any future byte-hashed fixture under `backend/conversions` has the same CRLF
  exposure as M1.
# S4-02b phase 2a: questions and fail-closed choices (Implementer, 2026-10-14)

The packet, the decisions and the phase-1 review did not settle the points below. For each one the Implementer took the conservative, fail-closed option and continued. None blocks phase 2b.

## Q1. Four currentness states, not one `STALE/PENDING`
The read returns `currentness.state` as one of:
- `CURRENT`: the head's consequence basis equals a fresh derivation;
- `STALE`: a head exists but its basis differs, with reasons `PRICE_SELECTION_CHANGED`, `TECHNICAL_INPUT_CHANGED` or `BASIS_CHANGED`;
- `PENDING`: no consequence yet, with reason `NOT_YET_COMPUTED` or `NOT_YET_COMPUTED_NO_WORK_RECORDED`;
- `HISTORICAL`: the FindingRevision is not the latest.

Only a `CURRENT` head is `presented_as_current`. The bridge shows an amount only for a CURRENT PRICED head.

**Ruling needed:** whether the UI should merge `STALE` and `PENDING` into the packet's single "STALE/PENDING" label. Phase 2b can map them either way.

## Q2. Currentness is a fresh derivation at read time, not a price-only comparison
The read runs `load_input` + `quantify` + the window price selection for the selected latest FindingRevision, then compares the full consequence basis with the head's. After M1, that basis holds the selected price revision, or the window-clipped price boundaries, plus the technical digest.

Price-only comparison would miss one case. A late-accepted power reading inside the window changes the technical input, yet no work row is enqueued for evidence acceptance (0011 triggers fire only on price publication and FindingRevision insert). The head is then shown `STALE (TECHNICAL_INPUT_CHANGED)`, never as current, until a recompute runs.

**Ruling needed:** whether evidence acceptance should also enqueue consequence work. That would be a new trigger on `accepted_evidence`, outside this phase.

## Q3. Replay of a non-head basis (phase-1 Q6)
An unchanged basis replays the stored ID. If an exact earlier state ever recurred (A, then B, then A), `append` would return revision 1 while the head is revision 2. The read would then report `STALE` until a different state appends.

This is fail-closed, and in practice unreachable:
- price revisions and FindingRevisions are append-only with fresh IDs;
- the M1 basis carries revision IDs, so a "return" to an earlier price is always a new revision ID.

## Q4. Pre-0011 data (review L4): both options taken
- **Bootstrap.** Migration 0011 enqueues one `FINDING_REVISION_APPENDED` work row for every FindingRevision that exists when it runs. This is the same row the trigger would have written. The test downgrades to 0010 and re-upgrades with Findings present.
- **Defence in depth.** The read reports "no consequence" as `PENDING`, never as absent or zero. If no work row exists either, the reason is `NOT_YET_COMPUTED_NO_WORK_RECORDED`.

Price revisions are not backfilled. The Finding work prices with the current timeline, which already includes them.

## Q5. Work targets
- `FINDING_REVISION_APPENDED` recomputes that exact revision, even if a later one has superseded it. Per the packet, "a new FindingRevision produces its own linked consequence."
- `ASSUMPTION_PUBLISHED` recomputes only each overlapping Finding's **latest** revision. Superseded revisions keep their consequences as history and are not repriced.

## Q6. The worker is a one-shot CLI, not a daemon
There is no in-process scheduler:
- `python -m assetops_backend.recompute_consequences run --site KOBO-001` drains the Site's work;
- `pending` lists what is left.

Between a publication and the next run, the read shows `STALE`, never the old amount as current.

Failure handling:
- A failed item records a typed attempt, one of `STORE_UNAVAILABLE`, `FINDING_REVISION_NOT_FOUND` or `RECOMPUTE_FAILED`, and stays pending.
- A concurrent worker that finds the work already SUCCEEDED records nothing (`skip_if_succeeded`).
- There is no retry cap.

**Ruling needed:** whether a deployment needs a scheduled runner. That is an operations choice.

## Q7. Bridge shape changed
`GET .../findings/{id}/financials` no longer returns the S4-02a placeholder:
- `consequence.amount` is `{low, high}` display strings, or `null`;
- new fields: `currentness`, `consequence_revision_id`, `quantity_reason`, `price_reason`;
- with no consequence store configured, `state` is `UNAVAILABLE` (typed, no amount) rather than a 503 for the whole bridge.

The S4-02a frontend types (`frontend/src/sites/financialsClient.ts`) are not updated; that is phase 2b. Until 2b lands, the S4-02a Financials screen (`SiteFinancials.tsx:481`) words every state other than `QUANTITY_UNAVAILABLE` as "No price for the whole window". That includes `PRICED`, `STALE`, `PENDING` and `UNAVAILABLE`, and it is wrong for all of them. The S4-02a UI must therefore not be shown over a 2a database before 2b.

## Q8. Display rounding
- **Minor units:** a fixed ISO 4217 table of 33 currencies.
- **Unknown currency:** gives `display.state = MINOR_UNIT_UNKNOWN` with no rounded amount, and the bridge shows no amount. The unrounded value stays in `amount`.
- **Quantity:** displayed rounded up to 0.01 L (`quantity.high_display`).
- **Text form:** the unrounded `amount.high` text is the stored numeric's text, so the scale may vary (`3.5200`). Clients compare it as a Decimal.

## Q9. Demonstration producer database
`tools/s402b_power_cases.py` runs the unchanged S4 producer plus P01-power. It runs into a database of its own, default `assetops_s402bcases`, and refuses any name that does not start with `assetops_s402b`. It never touches `assetops_s4cases`.

The demonstration day is 2026-10-23, which is clear of every S4 case day.
# S4-02b phase 2b: questions and conservative choices (Implementer, 2026-10-14)

None blocks review. Each has a built, conservative default.

## Q10. Closed-source state name (review M1)
Built: a fifth currentness state `AUDIT_ONLY` (reason `SOURCE_CLOSED`, plus `FINDING_REVISION_SUPERSEDED` when the revision is also old). It takes precedence over HISTORICAL/STALE/CURRENT and never derives. The bridge gives `AUDIT_ONLY` with no amount. Alternative: HISTORICAL with reason `SOURCE_CLOSED`. A separate state keeps "earlier version" and "source not open" distinguishable in the copy.

## Q11. How the demonstration Finding is labelled
Nothing in the evidence records which batch was authored for the demonstration (P01-power is posted with the registered gateway credential, like every S4 case). Built: `backend/assetops_backend/demonstration_label.py` reads the spec `conversions/s4-power-demo-cases-v1.json` and labels a Finding whose window lies wholly inside a declared case day at the declared Site (KOBO-001, 2026-10-23). It is a presentation label only; it changes no number or state. Risk: any later KOBO-001 Finding on 2026-10-23 would also be labelled. Better long-term: record authorship (a batch or source label) at ingestion; a Planner/Architect item, linked to the owner backlog "analysis input data for simulated and real Sites".

## Q12. Consequence versions on Assumptions
The packet says the Assumptions History view "shows exact price revisions and consequence revisions". Consequence revisions are per FindingRevision and there is no Site-wide consequence list read in 2a. Built: Financials â€º Overview lists every consequence version of the selected Finding version, each linking its exact price version on Assumptions; Assumptions lists price versions only. A Site-level "fuel cost versions that used this price" list needs a new read; deferred (G-05 itemization / later slice).

## Q13. STALE and PENDING copy (2a Q1)
Kept separate: "Out of date: no current cost" and "Not worked out yet". The STALE reason says "a recalculation is queued" only when the read lists pending work (review L4), else "has not been recalculated yet". Owner checkpoint question 4.

## Q14. PRICE_SPANS_WINDOW in the walkthrough
Not staged: it needs a test-scoped correction inside P01's window, which is not a fixture step. It is covered by `test_a_correction_inside_the_window_appends_an_unpriced_revision` (Postgres) and the frontend `UNPRICED / PRICE_SPANS_WINDOW` case. The walkthrough's UNPRICED example is `NO_PRICE` (empty store after recompute).

## Q15. Pinned consequence view keeps the head panel
With `consequence_revision_id`, the page shows the current (head) money panel first and the pinned earlier version in its own panel labelled "kept for the record". Alternative: replace the head panel. Keeping both makes the old/new comparison visible and never lets the old amount stand alone as if current.

## Verdict: ACCEPT with carries

All of the phase-1 carries landed, and none of them is vacuous. I confirmed each one by mutation or by reproducing it:

- **L1 to L7** all landed.

The worker is durable:
- a failure becomes a typed FAILED attempt and the item stays pending;
- a retry succeeds;
- a crash after the append replays the same ID.

Currentness never presents a stale or old amount as current *within its own model*. Display rounding is outward. The

## Focus areas

- **Recompute durability** (`recompute.py:102-125`):
  - Every exception becomes a typed FAILED attempt, and the item stays pending (no SUCCEEDED attempt).
  - If `record_attempt` itself fails, the run aborts, and the next run replays through the idempotent append. This is
    tested.
  - There is no retry cap and no backoff (Q6), which is acceptable for a one-shot CLI.
- **Currentness** (`consequence_read.py:157-183`):
  - CURRENT requires the head's full basis digest to equal a fresh derivation. Because the basis includes the amount,
    a CURRENT head's amount is by construction the amount the inputs give now.
  - The read takes the chain snapshot and the derivation in separate transactions. Every interleaving I could construct
    produces a false STALE, never a false CURRENT, except one microsecond window. In that window a technical input
    changes between `load_input` and the price snapshot, and no work row exists for that change anyway (Q2).
  - HISTORICAL never derives and never presents as current: verified by probe, but untested (M2).
- **Rounding** (`consequence_read.py:49-66`):
  - The low bound uses `ROUND_FLOOR` and the high bound `ROUND_CEILING`, to the ISO minor unit.
  - The 33-entry table is correct for the 0- and 3-digit currencies listed.
  - An unknown currency gives no display amount, and the bridge shows none.
  - `quantity.high_display` is ceilinged to 0.01 L.
- **Route** (`finding_api.py:245-277`):
  - Scope and access come first.
  - A store that is absent gives 503 (tested). A store that fails also gives 503, through `consequence_down`; this is
    untested at route level, but the bridge degrades to `UNAVAILABLE` (tested).
  - A bad or foreign ID gives 404 and never falls back (tested, and my mutation was killed).
- **Owner demonstration case:**
  - It is labelled `AUTHORED S4-02b DEMONSTRATION CASE`.
  - `s4-finding-cases-v1` is untouched: it is not in the diff.
  - Its day is clear of every S4 case day (tested).
  - The producer posts over TLS with the registered gateway credential into its own `assetops_s402b*` DB, and refuses
    any other name.
  - The arithmetic checks: 23 Ã— 5 min Ã— 20 kW Ã— 0.3 Ã— 1.10 = 12.65 L; Ã— 1.25 = 15.8125, and Ã— 1.28 = 16.192.
  - The Postgres test uses the in-process submit path. Only the producer exercises TLS.
- **Deviations:**
  - The bridge shape change breaks the S4-02a wording. `SiteFinancials.tsx:481` would label a PRICED head "No price for
    the whole window" next to a "Modelled potentialâ€¦" reason. It never shows an amount, so it is wrong but not
    over-claiming. Acceptable *only* because the branch is unmerged and 2b replaces it. The branch must not merge
    without 2b.
  - Evidence acceptance enqueues no work: see Q2.

## Findings

### M1 (Medium, fix first in 2b, backend): under a closed S3 source gate the amount is presented as current

- **Where.**
  - `finding_api.py:262-264` and `:311` pass only `finding_revision_is_latest` to the read.
  - `consequence_read.py:182` sets `presented_as_current = is_head and state == "CURRENT"`.
  - `bridge_consequence` (`finding_api.py:336-358`) returns `currentness: "CURRENT"` and an amount.
- **Observed.** `assetops_s402brev2_cases_test` has no Performance comparison report, so the gate is closed. An exact
  `revision_id` read of P01-power returns all of the following:
  - top-level `audit_only.non_current: true` ("this recorded revision is not a current claim");
  - `currentness.state: "CURRENT"`, `current_amount_shown: true` and `consequence.presented_as_current: true`;
  - a bridge with `currentness: "CURRENT"` and amount `{0.00, 15.82}`.
- **Why it matters.**
  - The handoff tells 2b: "Show the amount only when `consequence.presented_as_current`". A UI following that rule
    would present a money amount as current on a Finding the same response declares audit-only.
  - The packet requires a typed closed-source state in this read.
  - The Architect requires that a pinned row "never masquerades as current".
- **Fix.**
  - Pass `source["permitted"]` into `ConsequenceReadService.read`.
  - When the source is closed, give currentness `AUDIT_ONLY` (or `HISTORICAL` with reason `SOURCE_CLOSED`), set
    `presented_as_current` false, and make the bridge return that typed state with no amount.
  - Add a route test with a closed gate and an exact `revision_id`.

### M2 (Medium, test gap): the HISTORICAL branch is unprotected

- **Where.** `consequence_read.py:157-159`.
- **What is wrong.** Replacing the condition with `if False` passes all 30 tests in the recompute, store and API suites.
  The only HISTORICAL coverage is a stubbed bridge test.
- **Why it matters.** Without it, the head of a superseded FindingRevision would be presented as current. The packet
  names this contract: "Old pinned row is labelled historical".
- **Fix.** Add a Postgres test that appends a second FindingRevision, or reuses a multi-revision case. It must assert
  that the old revision's read is `HISTORICAL`, with `presented_as_current` false and no bridge amount.

### L1 (Low, test gap): the new route has no 401/403 or failing-store 503 test

The route reuses `open_scope`, and `consequence_call` maps `consequence_down`. Mirror the S4-02a bridge tests
(`test_s4_assumption_read_api.py:143-147`) for `/financial-consequences`.

### L2 (Low, test gap): the L6 assessment-completeness half of `financial_consequence_links_complete` is untested

- **Where.** `0011:306-311`.
- **What is wrong.** The test (`test_s4_consequence_store_postgres.py` "Review L6, at commit") clones a PRICED row, so it
  trips the PRICE-link RAISE first. Disabling the completeness RAISE survives. The test comment claims both halves.
- **Fix.** Add a NOT_EVALUATED or UNPRICED clone without assessment links, matched on "does not link every INTERVAL
  assessment".

### L3 (Low, test gaps): worker target selection and concurrent skip

- **Where.** `postgres_consequence_store.py:281` (latest-revision filter) and `:255` (`skip_if_succeeded`).
- **What is wrong.** Both mutations survive.
- **Why it matters.**
  - Without the filter, ASSUMPTION_PUBLISHED would reprice superseded FindingRevisions, which contradicts Q5.
  - Without the skip, two workers would each record a SUCCEEDED attempt. The unique index would then raise on the
    second, and that worker would convert the error into a FAILED record. This is harmless, but it is unproven.
- **Fix.** Add one test per item.

### L4 (Low): bridge STALE wording claims a recompute is under way

- **Where.** `finding_api.py:324`: "it is being recalculated".
- **What is wrong.** In the Q2 case (`TECHNICAL_INPUT_CHANGED` with no pending work), and with a one-shot CLI (Q6),
  nothing is necessarily queued.
- **Fix.** Word the state from `pending_work`, for example "will be recalculated" versus "needs recalculation". Settle
  this with the 2b copy.
- The pending-work list scoping (`postgres_consequence_store.py:336`) is also untested; the mutation survived. It affects
  only the listed `pending_work`, not the state.

| --- | --- | --- |

## Residual risk not named in the packet or handoff

- **Closed-gate walkthroughs.** A producer-template DB has no comparison report, so every Finding read is closed-gate
  and audit-only. That makes M1 the *default* walkthrough state, not an edge case. Either the 2b walkthrough opens the
  gate, or M1's fix shows audit-only money.
- **Cost per read.** Every bridge and read call runs a full `load_input` plus a price snapshot. This is fine at the
  scale of one Finding, but a list view over many Findings would multiply it.
- **Unmapped store errors.** `_run` maps only `OperationalError` and `ProgrammingError` to unavailable. Any other DB error
  inside a derive would surface as a 500 on the bridge, rather than as typed `UNAVAILABLE`.

   closed-source state. Show the amount only from `display.amount_low/high`, and only when `presented_as_current`.
4. Walkthrough: decide whether the gate is open (run the comparison) or whether money is shown as audit-only.
5. Send Q2 to the Architect in the milestone batch.

## Findings

### H1 (High, fix on branch before owner checkpoint): non-current states show the stored amount in the main money panel; the evidence "no amount" claims are vacuous

- **Where.**
  - `frontend/src/sites/FindingConsequence.tsx:445-451`. `FindingBridge` renders `BridgeTable rev={head}` and `HowWorkedOut` whenever a stored head exists, whatever the bridge state is.
  - `FindingConsequence.tsx:195-204`. The amount row prints `<strong>0.00 to X USD</strong>` for any PRICED revision; when `current` is false it adds "Recorded for this version; not the current cost".
  - The unit test enshrines this: `frontend/src/sites/__tests__/siteFinancials.test.tsx:539-571` expects `data-bridge-amount="recorded"` for STALE, HISTORICAL and AUDIT_ONLY.
  - The evidence claims are vacuous: `tools/s402b-fuel-cost-evidence.mjs:248` and `:303` assert only the outer `data-financials-amount="none"` attribute (the headline gate). They do not check the panel text.
- **Observed** in my run, screenshots `%TEMP%\claude\s402brev3-shots\s402b-audit-only-{desktop,narrow}.png` (the implementer's `s402b-audit-only-desktop.png` shows the same):
  - The closed gate shows "Audit material only ... not a current claim" and the headline "For the record only: no current cost".
  - Directly below that, "From finding to money: the stored version, not a current cost" shows **"0.00 to 16.20 USD"** in bold, at 1.28 USD per litre.
  - STALE (`s402b-stale-narrow.png`) shows the headline "Out of date: no current cost", then a bold **"0.00 to 15.82 USD"**. Two panels further down, "Price in use" shows 1.28 USD.
- **Why it matters.**
  - The 2a review's M1 asked that the closed-source bridge "return that typed state with no amount". The backend does this (`bridge_consequence`, tested), but the UI fetches the consequence read and restores the amount in the same panel.
  - The dispatch criterion is "no number shown for any non-CURRENT/PRICED state". The task requires that a lagging projection be "never a zero or old amount presented as current". The amount is labelled, but it is the most prominent figure in the panel, directly under a headline that says there is no current cost, and next to a "Price in use" that differs from it. That is exactly the misreading this contract exists to prevent, and the owner's client-facing guidance says money must not be over-read.
  - The packet's acceptance table ("audit-only captured ... no amount") and walkthrough step 6 ("For the record only: no current cost") tell the owner something the screen contradicts. That makes the walkthrough inaccurate for this step. Step 5 does mention the labelled stored figure for STALE.
- **Fix.** This is the conservative fix; any final copy goes to the owner.
  1. In the main "What this finding means in money" panel, when the bridge is not CURRENT PRICED, show no amount:
     - the amount row reads "No current cost";
     - the price row shows no stored price as if applied;
     - add a pointer to "Fuel cost versions" for the recorded figures.
     Alternatively, move the stored-version table into a closed disclosure titled for the record. Recorded amounts stay where the task requires them: the version list and the explicitly opened pinned version panel, both labelled "kept for the record".
  2. Under `AUDIT_ONLY`, also suppress amounts in the version list and the pinned panel unless the coordinator rules otherwise. The 2a carry and this dispatch both say "no amount anywhere" for the closed-source state. The S3 audit statement already says the revision is not a current claim.
  3. Change the evidence claims at `:248`, `:303` and the HISTORICAL claim to assert that `[data-financials-bridge]` innerText has no `\d+\.\d\d USD` (and, for AUDIT_ONLY, the whole `main`). Update `siteFinancials.test.tsx:539-571` to match.
  4. Correct walkthrough steps 5 and 6 to describe what is then shown.
- This is not an acceptance gap in the backend; it is a UI regression of a reviewed carry.

### L1 (Low, test gap): the Headline currentness guard is unprotected

- **Where.** `FindingConsequence.tsx:371`. Mutation F1 survives.
- **Why it matters.** The backend never sends `state: PRICED` with a non-CURRENT currentness today, so this is defence in depth. But it is the guard the packet cites.
- **Fix.** Add a case in which the bridge has `state: "PRICED"` with an amount and `currentness: "STALE"`, and assert that no headline amount is shown.

### L2 (Low, test gap): the closed-source notice on Financials is untested

- **Where.** `FindingConsequence.tsx:443`. Mutation F5 survives.
- **Fix.** Assert the `audit_only.statement` in the AUDIT_ONLY test case.

### L3 (Low): the demonstration label fails open

- **Where.**
  - `backend/assetops_backend/demonstration_label.py:29-33`. An unreadable or missing spec silently returns no declared cases.
  - `:51-53`. An open episode (`end` None) is never labelled.
- **Why it matters.** Either case shows authored demonstration money with no label, which is the one direction the owner ruling forbids.
- **Fix.** Make spec-load failure loud: raise, or have a test assert the shipped spec parses with at least one case. Decide whether an open episode on a declared day should be labelled; it should be. See also Q11.

### L4 (Low): the two reads can disagree on one screen

- **Where.** `FindingConsequence.tsx:524-536`; the test at `siteFinancials.test.tsx:555` asserts it.
- **What happens.** With the bridge UNAVAILABLE ("Could not be read") but the consequence read CURRENT, the version list labels a version "the current fuel cost" with its amount, while the headline says it could not be read. The same applies to the general race between the two independent reads (cost-per-read residual).
- **Fix.** Mark a history entry current only when the bridge's currentness is also CURRENT, or derive the headline from the consequence read.

### L5 (Low): the consequence history UI has no pager

- **Where.** `FindingConsequence.tsx:553-559`.
- **What happens.** The read pages at 200 (`PAGE_LIMIT`). The UI says "Showing N of total" but offers no "Show later versions", unlike Assumptions.
- **Why it is Low.** It is unreachable at demonstration scale. It is noted because the packet asks for "complete paged history".

Nothing else found. In particular, the 2b scan of new frontend copy shows no untagged "not available" literal. The orphan guard passes unchanged. Typed runtime states (`QUANTITY_WORDS`, `PRICE_WORDS`) are runtime reasons, not absent-capability copy.

**Q11 in detail.**
- **Why it is acceptable now.**
  - It is presentation-only and changes no state or amount (B3 shows only the label test fails).
  - It is Site- and day-scoped, and the spec days are tested clear of the S4 case days.
  - A false positive would label a non-demo KOBO-001 Finding wholly inside 2026-10-23 UTC as authored demonstration data. That is untrue but under-claims; it never presents demo money as real.
- **What is unsafe.** Only the fail-open cases in L3. Fix them on the branch.
- **Limits that must be stated plainly.**
  - The label is not tenant-scoped.
  - 2026-10-23 is nine days away. If the Lab's live KOBO-001 gateway produces S4 Findings on that UTC day in any database, they will carry a false "written by the AssetOps team" statement.
- **Architect/Planner item.** Record authorship (a batch or source label) at ingestion, and retire the date heuristic before any non-demonstration tenant, or before live Lab data can land on a declared day. Link it to the owner backlog item "analysis input data for simulated and real Sites".

**Q12 in detail.**
- The manifest claim for `assumptions:3#a` is "prior assumption and consequence revisions at selected cutoff".
- What is built:
  - price versions at a recorded cutoff on Assumptions;
  - consequence versions per Finding version on Overview, by exact ID, with no recorded-cutoff selection.
- The task text also places consequence revisions in "the Assumptions History view".
- Either the Architect rules that Overview plus exact-price links satisfy the claim, or `assumptions:3#a` is promoted only for its price portion and the consequence-at-cutoff part goes to G-05 or a later read.

    - the version list;
    - an explicitly opened earlier version, which adds "It is not the current cost".
  - **Can a STALE head's recorded amount in the list bring H1 back? No, in my judgement.**
    - At STALE the list shows "Version 1 Â· 0.00 to 15.82 USD, at 1.25 USD per litre Â· kept for the record"
      (screenshot `s402b-stale-desktop.png`). It is in a separate panel and is not bold.
    - It is never called current: `data-consequence-current="false"`, because `current` now also needs the bridge
      to be CURRENT.
    - The money panel above it says "Out of date: no current cost" and points to the list.
    - This is the placement the original review's fix allowed ("version list ... labelled 'kept for the record'").
    - The task requirement is that no old amount is *presented as current*, and it is not. See I2 for the copy.
  - **Is it right to keep "Price in use N.NN USD per litre" under AUDIT_ONLY? Yes.**
    - `PriceInUse` (`SiteFinancials.tsx:498-520`) reads the Site's assumptions at today's time. It does not depend on
      the Finding, the consequence read or the S3 source gate.
    - It is an input price, not a fuel cost, and it does not depend on the Finding's closed source.
  - **Can the exclusion `\d+\.\d\d USD(?! per litre)` hide a cost? Not with the code as it is.**
    - The pattern excludes only a figure followed directly by " per litre".
    - No cost formatter produces that. `range()` output ends in the currency; the list entry ends "USD, at ..."; in
      `textContent` the table amount cell is followed by "Recorded..." or "Modelled...".
    - The money-panel claims (`panelAmounts`) and the unit tests have no exclusion at all: they count any
      `\d+\.\d\d` or any `USD`, so a stray price or cost in the panel would also fail them.
    - See I4 for the limits of the pattern.
- **L1: fixed.** There is a new test, PRICED with currentness STALE. Dropping `c.currentness === "CURRENT"` from
  `Headline` makes it fail (1 failed).
- **L2: fixed.** The AUDIT_ONLY case asserts the `audit_only.statement`. Removing the notice makes it fail (1 failed).
- **L3: fixed.**
  - In `demonstration_label.py`, `_declared` raises `SpecUnreadable`. `lru_cache` does not cache the exception.
  - `demonstration_case` catches it and returns `{"label": "Source not confirmed", "case_id": "unverified", ...}`,
    which the existing `Demonstration` component shows through `demo.label`.
  - An open episode (`end` None) is labelled by the day of its start.
  - The fail-closed result applies to every Site while the spec cannot be read. That over-labels, which is the safe
    direction.
  - The new tests are: open episode, unreadable spec (monkeypatched `_declared`), and the shipped spec parses with at
    least one case. See I1 for a residual test gap.
- **L4: fixed.**
  - A history entry is "the current fuel cost" only when the bridge currentness *and* the consequence read are both
    CURRENT.
  - The new test uses an UNAVAILABLE bridge with a loaded history.
  - Reverting the fix makes 2 tests fail, including the updated `it.each` UNAVAILABLE case, which now expects
    `"false"`.
- **L5:** left as a carry, as the packet states. Unchanged.
- **Walkthrough.**
  - Step 5 (before recompute) and step 6 match my screenshots word for word: the pointer line, the list labelled
    "kept for the record", and Price in use at 1.28.
  - The new section "Not walked, and limits of the demonstration label" says plainly that PRICE_SPANS_WINDOW is tested

  is already named in the walkthrough and is an Architect item (Q11). No change from the original review.

# Architect rulings: S4-02b `fuel-cost-1` upper bound (2026-10-13)

Branch `plan/S4-02-refine`. These rulings answer Q1 in `.agent/planner-S4-02-questions-2026-10-13.md` and C4 in `.agent/S4-02-plan-review.md` together. They refine `D-2026-10-13-s4-bounded-financial-basis`; the addendum below makes the conditional bound and containment rule explicit.

## Q1 â€” model multiplier

**Ruling: A, conditionally.** Ratify the exact Decimal, dimensionless `sfc_upper_multiplier = 1.10` as an immutable parameter of the separately reviewed `fuel-cost-1` version. This is an *assumption of that model*: actual consumption per kWh is assumed no greater than `1.10 Ã—` the selected time-valid Foundation `specific-fuel-consumption` value. It is not a calibrated uncertainty interval, measured maximum, or imported `dispatch-2` constant. The numerical upper is a bound **only under this stated assumption and the C4 input rule below**. Do not show a positive upper before the parameter version and the input rule have independent review. If either fails, publish `QUANTITY_UNAVAILABLE` with null amount; do not silently substitute a point-rate or `SCENARIO` value.

The parameter version must record its exact Decimal value and unitless type; the inequality it asserts and its limitation; `fuel-cost-1` formula/version and immutable parameter ID or digest; the reviewed fixture/model-contract source, author/reviewer and review date or record; applicable Site/generator/period or explicit model scope; and the exact Foundation SFC revision/binding selection rule. Store the parameter version/ID, value, selected SFC revision IDs, accepted power interval IDs and canonical input digest on the consequence lineage. Name the 1.10 choice as a reviewed modelling allowance, **not** as empirical calibration; a later calibrated or Site-specific value needs a new version and review. It cannot be changed in place to reprice a pinned consequence.

**Exact S4-02b packet formula and wording:** `quantity_low = 0 L`; `quantity_high = 1.10 Ã— Î£_j (P_j,mean [kW] Ã— Î”t_j [h] Ã— SFC_j [L/kWh])`, over the distinct eligible accepted power intervals defined below, using unrounded Decimal values. The amount bound, when one compatible Site price covers the entire FindingRevision consequence window, is `amount_low = 0` and `amount_high = quantity_high Ã— price`, before outward display rounding. In the default UI use plain wording such as **â€œModelled potential fuel-cost exposure: 0 to [amount], under the reviewed fuel-cost-1 assumptions. This is not measured fuel saved or a physical maximum.â€** Put `fuel-cost-1`, multiplier, IDs, digest and the full assumption in details/lineage. The owner may settle final product copy at the planned S4-02 checkpoint; no new owner architecture choice is needed now.

## C4 â€” interval containment and duration

**Ruling: (i), fail-closed containment, with interval-once arithmetic.** `T_i` is the exact `[start,end)` duration of each stored `CANDIDATE` segment with `observed: true`; there is no `T_i,high`. Never use dispatch interval `upper_minutes`, `off_before` or `off_after` as Candidate duration. Build the union `C` of those Candidate spans for the exact selected FindingRevision, keeping generator asset identity. For a generator asset, select the complete accepted, good, nonnegative `GEN_POWER` mean intervals that intersect `C`. Each selected interval `[a_j,b_j)` must lie wholly within `C` for that asset, and their union must cover `C` without a gap. Distinct selected intervals must not overlap in time for the same asset; refuse ambiguous duplicate or conflicting readings rather than choosing or counting twice. The selected, time-valid Foundation SFC must be a single positive L/kWh value throughout each power interval, with unambiguous revision and binding provenance. If any condition fails for any Candidate span, the **whole Finding consequence** is `QUANTITY_UNAVAILABLE`, not a partial amount. A straddling interval that extends into Necessary, Indeterminate, unobserved or outside-Finding time fails even if its mean is accepted. Adjacent Candidate segments may form one contiguous span; an interval crossing their shared edge is eligible and is counted once.

**Exact S4-02b packet formula and wording:** For each eligible distinct interval `j`, set `Î”t_j = (b_j âˆ’ a_j)` in hours. Use `E_j = P_j,mean Ã— Î”t_j` as the accepted mean's full-interval kWh, then `quantity_high = Î£_j (E_j Ã— SFC_j Ã— 1.10) L` and `quantity_low = 0 L`. The containment test ensures the full-interval energy is only from observed Candidate time. Do not compute `max(mean) Ã— segment duration`: a mean can hide a higher-power part on one side of a segment cut. Do not split a straddling interval by assuming its mean applies uniformly to a subinterval. Store each counted interval once with its power value, duration, SFC revision, Candidate spans covered and overlap/containment result. This formula is a conditional model upper for potentially avoidable fuel; it makes no counterfactual saving claim.

**Unavailable fixtures S4-02b must add or retain:** missing accepted power; a positive-duration observed Candidate span with a coverage gap; a good power interval crossing a Candidateâ†’Necessary or Candidateâ†’Indeterminate edge (including the case where its mean times Candidate duration would understate energy); an interval extending into unobserved or outside-Finding time; overlapping or conflicting accepted power intervals; and missing, nonpositive, conflicting or mid-interval changing SFC/binding. Include a positive fixture in which one good interval crosses **only** the shared edge of two contiguous observed Candidate segments and is counted once. Also fixture an open dispatch interval with `upper_minutes = null` and complete contained Candidate power coverage: it remains quantifiable from stored Candidate endpoints, proving that `upper_minutes` is never used. Assert unavailable fixtures have no numeric quantity high or amount, and positive fixtures recompute from exact accepted IDs after reload.

The owner should see the bounded and straddling-unavailable cases in the scheduled S4-02 walkthrough and approve the visible explanation there. No separate owner decision is needed for this conservative source rule; it implements the already selected conditional financial basis.

Its version records the value, inequality, scope, source, reviewer/date, formula and immutable lineage; it is not a measured or calibrated physical bound. Count each distinct interval once: `quantity_low = 0 L`, `quantity_high = 1.10 Ã— Î£(mean kW Ã— full interval hours Ã— SFC L/kWh)`. Never use dispatch interval `upper_minutes`, a segment `max mean Ã— duration`, or prorating of an interval that crosses non-Candidate time. Any failed coverage, containment, power or SFC condition makes the whole consequence `QUANTITY_UNAVAILABLE` with no amount. # Codex Architect â€” A-S4-financial and G-02 missing-kind carry (2026-10-13)

The owner selected `A-S4-financial` in the 3.21% next-work contest. I resolve the architecture node with `D-2026-10-13-s4-bounded-financial-basis` below. This is an S4-02 implementation contract, not a priced Finding, a valuation of unassessed operation, or authorization for an estate roll-up.

## Assumptions, authority and time

- `FinancialAssumptionRevision` has an immutable ID, tenant ID, optional Site ID, stable `assumption_key`, monotonically increasing revision number per scope/key, ISO 4217 currency where money is involved, canonical unit, Decimal value, basis kind and source reference, half-open `[valid_from, valid_to)`, server `recorded_at` and supersession time/reference. The first S4-02 write/read is **Site scoped**; tenant-scope rows and precedence over Site rows require a later explicit rule. One currently known revision per Site/key may cover a given valid instant. A correction appends a revision and closes recorded knowledge of the prior one; it never changes the old value or a pinned consequence.
- The first priced key is `diesel_price_per_litre` in currency/L, with basis explicitly `AUTHORED_ESTIMATE`, `CONTRACT_RATE` or `RECORDED_PURCHASE` and evidence appropriate to that label. A typed `maintenance_rate_per_generator_hour` or `maintenance_cost_per_start` may be stored only with its own source/model and matching quantity; it cannot be folded into fuel or called actual maintenance expenditure from candidate minutes alone. A discretionary alternative price is `SCENARIO`, not the operational price. No default price, silent tenant fallback, implicit FX, negative rate or conversion from unlike units is allowed.
- The write route derives `actor_kind`, `actor_id`, `author_id` (human only), channel, authorization basis and `recorded_at` on the server under `A-actor-identity`. A client-supplied identity is ignored/refused, not trusted. If there is no authenticated human path, disable the human price edit; a labelled, server-authorized `SYSTEM` demo publication may use stable service-principal/job/fixture provenance but must not impersonate a person. Use an expected prior revision/idempotency key and reject conflicting validity or concurrent edits. A future attestation requires proof bound to exact content/version.
- Select a price valid for the **whole** assessed consequence window and known at the chosen recorded cutoff. A future-dated price does not reprice yesterday. The demo edit that reprices an old Finding must be an explicit, audited correction whose validity covers that old window; it creates a new assumption and consequence revision. If a window crosses a price boundary, S4-02 either uses a reviewed partition with separately cited quantities per segment or remains unpriced; this first slice must not silently use the current price for the full interval. Pinned IDs reproduce the old result.

## Quantity, range and consequence

- The source technical claim is one exact, currently supported mini-grid `FindingRevision` for candidate generator-support segments, with its selected primary and all supporting `AssessmentRevision` IDs, accepted public evidence, model/cohort, half-open period and Foundation/binding basis. `CANDIDATE_AVOIDABLE` minutes are an upper bound on potentially avoidable runtime, not liters saved. S3-01's current `dispatch-3.1` read explicitly withholds a fuel/money claim; S4-02 must review and version a separate `fuel-cost-1` derivation from accepted generator-power coverage during candidate segments and the selected Foundation's specific fuel consumption in L/kWh. Do not import legacy `dispatch-2` fuel outputs, use private Lab truth, infer constant load, interpolate missing intervals or price Indeterminate/Necessary minutes. If those inputs or a defensible model bound are absent, state quantity unavailable and show no amount.
- For the first supported case, retain **unrounded** nonnegative quantity bounds in liters and their evidence/configuration/formula digest. Zero is the lower bound on *potentially avoidable* fuel, because candidate does not prove the counterfactual; the upper bound is the reviewed model's fuel estimate for covered candidate operation under the stated configured rate/uncertainty. Label the result â€œmodelled, assumption-conditioned potential fuel-cost exposureâ€, never fuel actually saved, recovered value or a forecast that operation will change. If the rate has no defensible upper bound, do not pretend its point value is a physical upper bound: either publish an expressly conditional point-rate scenario with no hard-bound wording or leave the amount unavailable until the model supplies a reviewed bound. The Planner must fixture both the bounded and unavailable cases.
- `FinancialConsequenceRevision` is append-only under tenant/Site, Finding ID + exact FindingRevision ID, consequence type, selected assessment revision IDs, technical quantity bounds/unit/source digest, every assumption revision ID used, formula version, basis kind, overlap group, period, limitations, amount low/high and currency, plus a separate canonical basis digest. The technical design's single `quantity` field may retain a labelled reference amount, but the range needs explicit `quantity_low/high` (or an equivalent immutable typed bounds structure); no scalar is displayed as a proved saving. Recompute under a changed valid price appends a new consequence revision for the **same** FindingRevision and technical quantity/basis. It never mutates the Finding, assessment, evidence or old consequence. A changed FindingRevision needs a separately linked consequence. Identical basis digest is idempotent.
- Multiply compatible Decimal quantity bounds by the nonnegative price **before** display rounding. Store unrounded Decimal results; round displayed low downward and high upward to the currency's minor unit, and use unrounded compatible values for any permitted total. No mixed currency/unit, basis, window, overlap group, Finding revision or scenario/operational sum. This S4-02 slice should show the one consequence line and its exclusions, not a portfolio total. `UNPRICED` means a supported technical quantity with no valid price: amount and currency are null, not zero. `QUANTITY_UNAVAILABLE` means the technical derivation cannot be supported; `STALE/PENDING` means the consequence has not caught up with a selected Finding or assumption revision; an old revision is labelled historical/superseded. A pinned historical row remains inspectable with exact IDs and never masquerades as current.

## Storage, reads and screen boundary

S4-02 persists the two immutable revision families, their multi-assumption/assessment links, actor and basis metadata, and a recomputation/outbox marker or equivalent transactional dirty state so an assumption edit cannot leave an unlabelled stale current amount. It serves tenant/Site-scoped paged assumption current/history and Finding-selected consequence current/history reads with exact revision selectors, validity/recorded times, quantity/price/amount bounds, formula and lineage, typed `UNPRICED`, `QUANTITY_UNAVAILABLE`, `STALE/PENDING` and access/source failures. Reads link to the exact FindingRevision, assessment revisions, accepted Evidence and original envelopes through S4-03 rather than reconstructing provenance from display labels. A price write and consequence append are separate durable events; retries must not duplicate either.

The first S4-02 UI may claim `financials:1#a` (one supported bounded mini-grid fuel consequence or explicit unpriced/unavailable state), `financials:2#a` (assumptions actually used), `assumptions:1#a` (versioned Site price/currency/unit/author basis) and `assumptions:3#a` (prior assumption and consequence revisions at exact selection) **only after their own implementation and independent evidence**. The `financials:3` trade-off/compatibility claims, `assumptions:2` impact preview, second maintenance line without a reviewed source, cash flow, conversion, monthly extrapolation and `financial_scenario` remain named gaps or absent tabs. `mini:4#a` is S5 Overview composition of the same selected consequence/unpriced state, not an S4-02 screen claim; `mini:1â€“3` remain S5/Index work. The mock amounts and F-104 are illustrative.

`A-money-comparability` stays outside W1 ranking: it must later decide cross-option/Site windows, basis labels, currency conversion, coverage, overlap and source freshness before any estate total or unlike-line comparison. `financial_scenario` is a separate revisioned alternative with frozen inputs, baseline and `Scenario` label; it cannot overwrite this operational consequence or count as recovered money. Cold-chain and EV reuse assumption identity/time/actor, consequence revision/lineage, currency/unit compatibility, overlap, Unknown and historical selection; they must supply their own technical quantity, price kind and fixtures before making a monetary claim.

## Planner refinement and size

Split the outline into two reviewable **M** slices, not one broad S/M closeout: **S4-02a** delivers Site-scoped authenticated/system-authorized price publication, exact assumption history/UI and an honest unpriced/quantity-unavailable Finding bridge; **S4-02b** delivers the reviewed `fuel-cost-1` quantity fixture, consequence store/recompute/read/UI, price-change revision and complete lineage walkthrough. Keep one active slice at a time and make `I-S4-02`/W1 panel promotion a final closeout only after both, fresh reviews and the owner checkpoint. The Planner must specify migration constraints, valid/recorded-time selection, actor failure paths, accepted-power/consumption fixtures, conflicting/missing basis, backdated correction versus future price, half-open price boundary, Decimal/outward-rounding tests, idempotent retries and old/new Findingâ€“consequence drill-downs.

## G-02 C-c: missing-kind rule

`missing_kind` identifies the **first absent authority needed to make the claim**, not merely the eventual source. Use `contract` when the record type, who may author it, validity/meaning or read/decision rule is not yet agreed; use `data_source` when that contract exists but its real producer, feed or records are absent; `decision` is a pending reviewed product/portion choice over existing inputs, `build` an agreed unimplemented path, and `visual` an agreed claim needing presentation. A source may remain `absent` under a `contract` gap. The commissioning gap says no commissioning record type exists, so change `G-readiness-commissioning.missing_kind` from `data_source` to `contract` **now**, without altering its visible â€œNot recordedâ€ state, scope, status, allocation or W1 ranking. At W1-exit allocation, the owner still decides whether/when to establish the record and acquisition workflow; after a contract exists, a separately traceable missing producer may become a data-source gap. This clarifies only the commissioning example in `D-2026-10-12-mock-gap-closure-mechanism`.

No additional owner decision is needed to record this architecture contract: the owner already selected `A-S4-financial`. The later S4-02 owner checkpoint must approve the actual basis wording and visual distinction between modelled exposure, unpriced and historical money; any tenant-wide price policy, true maintenance cost, FX or scenario comparison requires its own reviewed owner choice.

Each publication carries a stored `change_kind`, shown in History. `INITIAL`: the first price for a Site/key, or one covering time with no current price; it may be backdated, and is refused with 409 if its range overlaps any current assignment. `SUCCESSION`: `valid_from` ≥ the server `recorded_at`; it may only truncate the single open-ended current assignment containing `valid_from`; any other overlap is refused with a typed 409. `CORRECTION`: backdated or bounded; requires a reason; supersedes the current assignments over its authored range and re-assigns the uncovered left/right remainders to their same old revisions. The transaction locks the Site/key series, rejects concurrent prior mismatch and conflicting retry payload, and returns the same revision ID on exact retry. After the transaction no two current assignments overlap (enforced by the item 1 constraint), and an overlap that `change_kind` does not permit is refused. S4-02a builds the human write route with no success path. It must not use `development_access`.

tasks/S4-02a-site-assumptions.md-16-## Persistence, publication and read contract
tasks/S4-02a-site-assumptions.md-17-
tasks/S4-02a-site-assumptions.md-18-1. New Postgres migration: `financial_assumption_revision` with UUID ID, tenant/Site FK (Site required in this slice), stable key `diesel_price_per_litre`, revision number, ISO currency, unit `currency/L`, explicitly authored nonnegative finite `numeric`/Decimal value, basis enum `AUTHORED_ESTIMATE|CONTRACT_RATE|RECORDED_PURCHASE`, source reference, authored `[valid_from,valid_to)` intent, server `recorded_at`, server actor kind/id, nullable human `author_id`, channel, authorization basis, service/job/fixture provenance and idempotency key. Unique `(tenant_id,site_id,key,revision_no)` and publication key. No tenant fallback, default price, negative rate, SCENARIO operational publication, or client-supplied actor/author. The price value/basis/source and authored validity are immutable. Record effective validity separately as assignment rows keyed to the price revision with `[valid_from,valid_to)` and `[recorded_from,recorded_to)`. Assignment rows are immutable except a set-once `recorded_to` (trigger-enforced, as `foundation_revision.superseded_at`). Constraint: `EXCLUDE USING gist (tenant_id WITH =, site_id WITH =, assumption_key WITH =, tstzrange(valid_from,valid_to,'[)') WITH &&, tstzrange(recorded_from,recorded_to,'[)') WITH &&)`. Superseded and new rows share one server instant (`recorded_to` = `recorded_from`), taken with `clock_timestamp()` after acquiring the series lock (not transaction-start `now()`); it must be later than the series' latest `recorded_from`, and every stored range must be non-empty (CHECK `valid_from < valid_to` where bounded, and `recorded_from < recorded_to` when set). A trigger (it spans two tables, so not a CHECK) requires every current assignment's valid range to lie inside its revision's authored range. Index both time axes. A future publication closes the old assignment at `t` and retains its original revision ID for earlier time. A middle-only correction supersedes the old assignment, records two current assignments of the **same old price revision** for the unaffected left/right spans, and assigns the corrected middle to the new revision. Historical assignments remain selectable at older recorded cutoffs. Thus a price row is never rewritten, a pinned consequence still finds its original price ID, and `STALE` arises only if the price revision selected for its entire window actually changes. This assignment table is the migration's explicit resolution of review carry M1 and the technical design's simplified single validity row.
tasks/S4-02a-site-assumptions.md:19:2. Publish only from a server-side CLI/job/fixture with a stable service principal, explicit Site, source, expected prior revision and idempotency key. The CLI is an administrative demo trigger, not an anonymous UI action; record SYSTEM/job/fixture provenance. Each publication carries a stored `change_kind`, shown in History. `INITIAL`: the first price for a Site/key, or one covering time with no current price; it may be backdated, and is refused with 409 if its range overlaps any current assignment. `SUCCESSION`: `valid_from` ≥ the server `recorded_at`; it may only truncate the single open-ended current assignment containing `valid_from`; any other overlap is refused with a typed 409. `CORRECTION`: backdated or bounded; requires a reason; supersedes the current assignments over its authored range and re-assigns the uncovered left/right remainders to their same old revisions. The transaction locks the Site/key series, rejects concurrent prior mismatch and conflicting retry payload, and returns the same revision ID on exact retry. After the transaction no two current assignments overlap (enforced by the item 1 constraint), and an overlap that `change_kind` does not permit is refused. S4-02a builds the human write route with no success path. Refusal order: 401 (no verified principal; the default access), then 422 (any body actor or author field), then 403/404 (tests inject these via the access seam); an admitted request without a verified human principal returns 401. It must not use `development_access`.
tasks/S4-02a-site-assumptions.md-20-3. `GET /api/sites/{site_code}/financial-assumptions?key=&valid_at=&recorded_at=&after_revision_no=&limit=` and `/financial-assumptions/{revision_id}` (equivalent Site-scoped routes acceptable) return exact current/at-recorded-cutoff selection, selected ID, bounded pagination/cursor, valid and recorded intervals, price/currency/unit, basis/source, actor/channel/provenance and explicit no-price or conflict/source-failure state. Half-open boundary: a price ending at `t` does not cover `t`; another beginning at `t` does. An exact ID is stable after later writes. Reuse Finding API's tenant/Site 401/403/404 and 503 semantics; a bad/foreign revision never falls back to latest. Never promise arbitrary S3 `knowledge_at` replay: the cutoff selects stored assumption knowledge only.
tasks/S4-02a-site-assumptions.md-21-4. The selected Finding route/Financials bridge holds Finding ID and exact FindingRevision ID, selected window and accepted-lineage link to S4-03. Before `fuel-cost-1` exists, display `QUANTITY_UNAVAILABLE`; when a separately supported technical quantity exists but no whole-window price, display `UNPRICED` with null amount/currency. These are typed states, not zero and not mock numbers. No consequence revision is manufactured in this slice. Closed Performance gate and pinned historical Finding follow S4-03 audit rules.

.agent/S4-02b-questions-2.md-1-# S4-02b phase 2a: questions and fail-closed choices (Implementer, 2026-10-14)
.agent/S4-02b-questions-2.md-2-
.agent/S4-02b-questions-2.md-3-The packet, the decisions and the phase-1 review did not settle the points below. For each one the Implementer took the conservative, fail-closed option and continued. None blocks phase 2b.
.agent/S4-02b-questions-2.md-4-
.agent/S4-02b-questions-2.md:5:## Q1. Four currentness states, not one `STALE/PENDING`
.agent/S4-02b-questions-2.md-6-The read returns `currentness.state` as one of:
.agent/S4-02b-questions-2.md-7-- `CURRENT`: the head's consequence basis equals a fresh derivation;
.agent/S4-02b-questions-2.md:8:- `STALE`: a head exists but its basis differs, with reasons `PRICE_SELECTION_CHANGED`, `TECHNICAL_INPUT_CHANGED` or `BASIS_CHANGED`;
.agent/S4-02b-questions-2.md-9-- `PENDING`: no consequence yet, with reason `NOT_YET_COMPUTED` or `NOT_YET_COMPUTED_NO_WORK_RECORDED`;
.agent/S4-02b-questions-2.md:10:- `HISTORICAL`: the FindingRevision is not the latest.
.agent/S4-02b-questions-2.md-11-
.agent/S4-02b-questions-2.md-12-Only a `CURRENT` head is `presented_as_current`. The bridge shows an amount only for a CURRENT PRICED head.
.agent/S4-02b-questions-2.md-13-
.agent/S4-02b-questions-2.md:14:**Ruling needed:** whether the UI should merge `STALE` and `PENDING` into the packet's single "STALE/PENDING" label. Phase 2b can map them either way.
.agent/S4-02b-questions-2.md-15-
.agent/S4-02b-questions-2.md:16:## Q2. Currentness is a fresh derivation at read time, not a price-only comparison
.agent/S4-02b-questions-2.md:17:The read runs `load_input` + `quantify` + the window price selection for the selected latest FindingRevision, then compares the full consequence basis with the head's. After M1, that basis holds the selected price revision, or the window-clipped price boundaries, plus the technical digest.
.agent/S4-02b-questions-2.md-18-
.agent/S4-02b-questions-2.md:19:Price-only comparison would miss one case. A late-accepted power reading inside the window changes the technical input, yet no work row is enqueued for evidence acceptance (0011 triggers fire only on price publication and FindingRevision insert). The head is then shown `STALE (TECHNICAL_INPUT_CHANGED)`, never as current, until a recompute runs.
.agent/S4-02b-questions-2.md-20-
.agent/S4-02b-questions-2.md:21:**Ruling needed:** whether evidence acceptance should also enqueue consequence work. That would be a new trigger on `accepted_evidence`, outside this phase.
.agent/S4-02b-questions-2.md-22-
.agent/S4-02b-questions-2.md-23-## Q3. Replay of a non-head basis (phase-1 Q6)
.agent/S4-02b-questions-2.md:24:An unchanged basis replays the stored ID. If an exact earlier state ever recurred (A, then B, then A), `append` would return revision 1 while the head is revision 2. The read would then report `STALE` until a different state appends.
.agent/S4-02b-questions-2.md-25-
.agent/S4-02b-questions-2.md-26-This is fail-closed, and in practice unreachable:
.agent/S4-02b-questions-2.md:27:- price revisions and FindingRevisions are append-only with fresh IDs;
.agent/S4-02b-questions-2.md-28-- the M1 basis carries revision IDs, so a "return" to an earlier price is always a new revision ID.
.agent/S4-02b-questions-2.md-29-
.agent/S4-02b-questions-2.md-30-## Q4. Pre-0011 data (review L4): both options taken
.agent/S4-02b-questions-2.md:31:- **Bootstrap.** Migration 0011 enqueues one `FINDING_REVISION_APPENDED` work row for every FindingRevision that exists when it runs. This is the same row the trigger would have written. The test downgrades to 0010 and re-upgrades with Findings present.
.agent/S4-02b-questions-2.md-32-- **Defence in depth.** The read reports "no consequence" as `PENDING`, never as absent or zero. If no work row exists either, the reason is `NOT_YET_COMPUTED_NO_WORK_RECORDED`.
.agent/S4-02b-questions-2.md-33-
.agent/S4-02b-questions-2.md-34-Price revisions are not backfilled. The Finding work prices with the current timeline, which already includes them.
.agent/S4-02b-questions-2.md-35-
.agent/S4-02b-questions-2.md-36-## Q5. Work targets
.agent/S4-02b-questions-2.md:37:- `FINDING_REVISION_APPENDED` recomputes that exact revision, even if a later one has superseded it. Per the packet, "a new FindingRevision produces its own linked consequence."
.agent/S4-02b-questions-2.md:38:- `ASSUMPTION_PUBLISHED` recomputes only each overlapping Finding's **latest** revision. Superseded revisions keep their consequences as history and are not repriced.
.agent/S4-02b-questions-2.md-39-
.agent/S4-02b-questions-2.md-40-## Q6. The worker is a one-shot CLI, not a daemon
.agent/S4-02b-questions-2.md-41-There is no in-process scheduler:
.agent/S4-02b-questions-2.md:42:- `python -m assetops_backend.recompute_consequences run --site KOBO-001` drains the Site's work;
.agent/S4-02b-questions-2.md-43-- `pending` lists what is left.
.agent/S4-02b-questions-2.md-44-
.agent/S4-02b-questions-2.md:45:Between a publication and the next run, the read shows `STALE`, never the old amount as current.
.agent/S4-02b-questions-2.md-46-
.agent/S4-02b-questions-2.md-47-Failure handling:
.agent/S4-02b-questions-2.md-48-- A failed item records a typed attempt, one of `STORE_UNAVAILABLE`, `FINDING_REVISION_NOT_FOUND` or `RECOMPUTE_FAILED`, and stays pending.
.agent/S4-02b-questions-2.md-49-- A concurrent worker that finds the work already SUCCEEDED records nothing (`skip_if_succeeded`).
.agent/S4-02b-questions-2.md-50-- There is no retry cap.
--
.agent/S4-02b-questions-2.md-55-`GET .../findings/{id}/financials` no longer returns the S4-02a placeholder:
.agent/S4-02b-questions-2.md-56-- `consequence.amount` is `{low, high}` display strings, or `null`;
.agent/S4-02b-questions-2.md-57-- new fields: `currentness`, `consequence_revision_id`, `quantity_reason`, `price_reason`;
.agent/S4-02b-questions-2.md-58-- with no consequence store configured, `state` is `UNAVAILABLE` (typed, no amount) rather than a 503 for the whole bridge.
.agent/S4-02b-questions-2.md-59-
.agent/S4-02b-questions-2.md:60:The S4-02a frontend types (`frontend/src/sites/financialsClient.ts`) are not updated; that is phase 2b. Until 2b lands, the S4-02a Financials screen (`SiteFinancials.tsx:481`) words every state other than `QUANTITY_UNAVAILABLE` as "No price for the whole window". That includes `PRICED`, `STALE`, `PENDING` and `UNAVAILABLE`, and it is wrong for all of them. The S4-02a UI must therefore not be shown over a 2a database before 2b.
.agent/S4-02b-questions-2.md-61-
.agent/S4-02b-questions-2.md-62-## Q8. Display rounding
.agent/S4-02b-questions-2.md-63-- **Minor units:** a fixed ISO 4217 table of 33 currencies.
.agent/S4-02b-questions-2.md-64-- **Unknown currency:** gives `display.state = MINOR_UNIT_UNKNOWN` with no rounded amount, and the bridge shows no amount. The unrounded value stays in `amount`.
.agent/S4-02b-questions-2.md-65-- **Quantity:** displayed rounded up to 0.01 L (`quantity.high_display`).

.agent/S4-02b-phase-2a-review.md-114-## Focus areas
.agent/S4-02b-phase-2a-review.md-115-
.agent/S4-02b-phase-2a-review.md:116:- **Recompute durability** (`recompute.py:102-125`):
.agent/S4-02b-phase-2a-review.md-117-  - Every exception becomes a typed FAILED attempt, and the item stays pending (no SUCCEEDED attempt).
.agent/S4-02b-phase-2a-review.md-118-  - If `record_attempt` itself fails, the run aborts, and the next run replays through the idempotent append. This is
.agent/S4-02b-phase-2a-review.md-119-    tested.
.agent/S4-02b-phase-2a-review.md-120-  - There is no retry cap and no backoff (Q6), which is acceptable for a one-shot CLI.
.agent/S4-02b-phase-2a-review.md-121-- **Currentness** (`consequence_read.py:157-183`):
.agent/S4-02b-phase-2a-review.md-122-  - CURRENT requires the head's full basis digest to equal a fresh derivation. Because the basis includes the amount,
.agent/S4-02b-phase-2a-review.md-123-    a CURRENT head's amount is by construction the amount the inputs give now.
.agent/S4-02b-phase-2a-review.md-124-  - The read takes the chain snapshot and the derivation in separate transactions. Every interleaving I could construct
.agent/S4-02b-phase-2a-review.md:125:    produces a false STALE, never a false CURRENT, except one microsecond window. In that window a technical input
.agent/S4-02b-phase-2a-review.md:126:    changes between `load_input` and the price snapshot, and no work row exists for that change anyway (Q2).
.agent/S4-02b-phase-2a-review.md-127-  - HISTORICAL never derives and never presents as current: verified by probe, but untested (M2).
.agent/S4-02b-phase-2a-review.md-128-- **Rounding** (`consequence_read.py:49-66`):
.agent/S4-02b-phase-2a-review.md-129-  - The low bound uses `ROUND_FLOOR` and the high bound `ROUND_CEILING`, to the ISO minor unit.
.agent/S4-02b-phase-2a-review.md-130-  - The 33-entry table is correct for the 0- and 3-digit currencies listed.
.agent/S4-02b-phase-2a-review.md-131-  - An unknown currency gives no display amount, and the bridge shows none.

.agent/S4-02b-questions-3.md-7-
.agent/S4-02b-questions-3.md:8:## Q11. How the demonstration Finding is labelled
.agent/S4-02b-questions-3.md-9-Nothing in the evidence records which batch was authored for the demonstration (P01-power is posted with the registered gateway credential, like every S4 case). Built: `backend/assetops_backend/demonstration_label.py` reads the spec `conversions/s4-power-demo-cases-v1.json` and labels a Finding whose window lies wholly inside a declared case day at the declared Site (KOBO-001, 2026-10-23). It is a presentation label only; it changes no number or state. Risk: any later KOBO-001 Finding on 2026-10-23 would also be labelled. Better long-term: record authorship (a batch or source label) at ingestion; a Planner/Architect item, linked to the owner backlog "analysis input data for simulated and real Sites".

tasks/README.md:100:This is a cutover map, not permission to delete historical fixtures or completed task evidence. The technical design's demo-to-Postgres comparison must pass per supported read: Evidence changes in S2-02 after evidence comparison; Performance changes in S3-01 after dispatch comparison. S2-01b makes the gateway durable, S2-01c exposes diagnostics/faults, and S2-02 makes upload and central acceptance real; S3 consumes durable recompute jobs. Do not call the current in-process publisher or JSON ingestion the production gateway/ledger.
tasks/README.md-101-
tasks/README.md-102-All packets inherit the current architecture and technical design. Every closeout needs the selected-window screen, read API, storage/reload, missing or contradictory evidence state, and lineage to the accepted SourceEnvelope. S2 additionally proves loss/retry/duplicate/conflict; S3–S4 preserve revisions after late evidence; S6 proves a failed guardrail. Follow `.ai/WORKFLOW.md` for independent review and appropriate checks. The four owner-review moments are S2 evidence, S4 bounded money, S6 verification and S7 Site Health. Global indexes, extra domains and empty tabs follow the Site journey.

frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp.md-6-
frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp.md-7-The [v6.9 interactive mock screens](../../mockups/v69/index.html) ask one question across Portfolio, Site, Performance, Findings, Evidence, Financials, Assets and Work: **what happened, what did it cost or put at risk, what should we do, and what evidence supports that answer?** AssetOps must connect physical operation to cost, revenue, reliability, maintenance burden and asset-life risk without turning missing evidence into false certainty. The Lab demonstrates the source of observations; it is not the source of product conclusions.
frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp.md-8-
frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp.md:9:The initial usable journey is **Portfolio priority → Site Overview → domain Performance → Finding → financial consequence → Action → later verification**, with an evidence drawer at every claim. A period is explicit and retained through drill-down; each claim retains its own assessed period and revision. Financial cards identify currency, basis, assumptions, uncertainty and overlap. “Unknown” includes a reason. Counts, health, and verified value are never inferred from silence or an action being marked complete.
