frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp.md:20:  A --> F[Finding and consequence revisions]
frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp.md-21-  F --> R[Read models / APIs]
frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp.md-22-  R --> U[Shared operator UI]
frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp.md-23-  P --> R
frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp.md-24-  L[Private Lab store] -. inspected only in Lab .-> W
frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp.md-25-```
frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp.md-26-
frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp.md:27:**Invariant:** replace the simulated gateway with a real gateway that emits equivalent `SourceEnvelope`s and the ingestion, analytics, read models and operator UI remain unchanged. Only explicit source provenance and the Lab entry point differ. At the first real pilot, a contract test must run a real capture or labelled vendor-format fixture through the physical path and compare normalized envelopes and downstream claims with the simulated path. The S2 demo proves the simulated path only.
frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp.md-28-
frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp.md-29-## Decisions and boundaries
frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp.md-30-
frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp.md-31-| Decision | Rationale and why now | Deliberately deferred |
frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp.md-32-| --- | --- | --- |
frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp.md-33-| One Python/FastAPI modular monolith, React operator UI, Postgres product database. Separate gateway process and gated Lab process. | Current stack already proves the journey; one transactional backend is fast to build and inspect. Separate processes enforce the source boundary. | Microservices, Kubernetes, generic event platform. |
frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp.md:34:| Versioned canonical envelope at the gateway; central ingestion is the sole acceptance authority. | Physical and simulated sources need exactly one public contract, with per-record refusal and provenance. | Edge Findings, edge financial analysis, edge portfolio decisions. |
frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp.md-35-| Direct authenticated HTTPS batch upload with durable edge SQLite outbox. | A pilot can tolerate intermittent connectivity without an always-on subscription or site broker. Batches are replayable and auditable. | MQTT broker, Kafka, dedicated stream processor. |
frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp.md-36-| Postgres for time-valid configuration, accepted evidence, claim revisions, work and read projections; object storage for large sealed artifacts. | Joins, transactions, revision history and multi-site reads are product requirements now. Artifact bytes need integrity and cheap retention, not relational querying. | Time-series database, data lake, distributed cache. |
frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp.md-37-| Small domain seam: `site_type` + versioned semantic role bindings + one analytic/read-model module per domain. | Mini-grid, cold-chain and EV share workflow but need different proof and language. | Plugin discovery, user-authored analytic DSL, universal asset ontology. |
--
frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp.md-46-
frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp.md-47-### Ingestion and transport
frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp.md-48-
frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp.md:49:Use `POST /api/ingestion/batches` over TLS with gateway credentials scoped to site/source, bounded batch size, rate limits and a queryable batch receipt. Authenticate the declared site and gateway against registered identity; do not trust `source_mode` or mapping/version references merely because they are present in JSON. Resolve the mapping and Foundation version effective **at source time**, including old versions after edits. Validate schema, clocks, units, role binding and provenance, then atomically persist acceptance decision, raw envelope digest/reference, normalized accepted record or rejection/conflict, and batch progress in Postgres. Unique keys for `(site, gateway, source, message_id)` and `(site, batch_id)` make retries idempotent; same key and different bytes is a conflict, not overwrite. Return per-envelope disposition and a receipt only after commit. Do not let a malformed sibling hide valid records. An outbox/work table in the same transaction schedules analytic recomputation; a worker leases jobs, retries safely and publishes revisioned read models. The UI exposes ingestion lag so “no finding yet” is not interpreted as healthy.
frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp.md-50-
frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp.md-51-Direct HTTPS is sufficient while pilots have tens of sites, bounded upload volume, and workers can keep up with Postgres. Upgrade to MQTT when device/site topology or near-real-time command/control requires brokered sessions; add a managed queue between receipt and workers when sustained ingest spikes or independent consumer fan-out exceed Postgres job capacity; adopt streaming infrastructure only when measured throughput/latency/replay demands it. These change transport or processing, not the envelope or acceptance semantics.
frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp.md-52-
--
frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp.md-54-
frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp.md-55-Keep explicit relational entities: `Tenant/Site`, `Asset` (stable physical identity), `FoundationRevision` (valid-time, recorded-time, topology, objectives, controls), `SignalBindingRevision`, `Gateway/Source`, `IngestionBatch`, `AcceptedEvidence`, `EvidenceRejection`, `AnalyticRun/AssessmentRevision`, `Finding` + immutable `FindingRevision`, `FinancialAssumptionRevision`, `FinancialConsequenceRevision`, `Action/ActionEvent`, `VerificationPlanRevision/Outcome`. Evidence retains raw digest, original and normalized value/unit, timestamps, quality, mapping/configuration revision, and provenance. No retroactive rewrite of a past basis: backdated corrections create a new revision and re-evaluation with links to both versions.
frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp.md-56-
frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp.md:57:Analytics are pure, versioned functions of accepted evidence, time-valid public configuration and declared assumption versions. Each output records evidence IDs/window, model version, claim level, gaps/alternatives and input digest. A Finding has stable identity and separate lifecycle events; changed evidence creates a revision, not a new indistinguishable row. A consequence names technical driver, quantity, formula, currency, assumption revision, measured/modelled/extrapolated/scenario basis and overlap group. Never add incompatible currencies, windows or overlapping causes. Verification compares a later accepted window with the plan's target **and** service/asset guardrails; return Verified, Ineffective or Inconclusive, with later recurrence able to reopen the Finding.
frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp.md-58-
frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp.md-59-Use Postgres partitions/indexes on `(site_id, source_time)` if real evidence volume warrants them; ordinary indexed tables are enough initially. Read projections can be Postgres tables/materialized views or bounded query services. Cache only reproducible, keyed views by site/window/domain/revision and invalidate on late evidence, changed configuration or analytic version. The database is the source of truth; a cache is never a second authority.
frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp.md-60-
--
frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp.md-66-| Reporting | Device/PLC/BMS/meter samples, gaps, bias, events | Simulated devices sample the world with cadence, gaps, bias and faults |
frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp.md-67-| Gateway | Protocol adapters, mappings, SQLite outbox, HTTPS | Same gateway contract and SQLite outbox; virtual device adapter and faultable link |
frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp.md-68-| Crossing | `SourceEnvelope` batch | Same `SourceEnvelope` batch, `SIMULATED` provenance |
frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp.md:69:| Product | Shared ingestion → Postgres → analytics → APIs → UI | Exactly the same |
frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp.md-70-
frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp.md-71-The world owns physics, controls, scenario causes and oracle expectations; simulated devices own only what their real counterparts could report. The simulated gateway cannot read private world state except through device reports. Its SQLite store persists staged envelopes and upload state exactly as a physical gateway would. Preserve Lab Draft/Stage/Commit only as a **release gate**: Draft may execute and inspect private truth; Stage seals public envelopes; Commit authorizes delivery through the same batch API and records a manifest/receipt. Commit cannot write evidence, Findings or site projections. Private truth, controller intent, causes, oracle and run controls live in a separate Lab store and Lab-only API with access controls; no foreign key or import from normal analytics to those objects. Simulated provenance may label a claim, never alter its calculation.
frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp.md-72-

frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp.md-76-
frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp.md-77-| Screen / API family (window and revision are explicit) | Read model and source | Domain difference |
frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp.md-78-| --- | --- | --- |
frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp.md:79:| Portfolio `GET /portfolio` | Site assessment coverage, ranked Finding index, consequence rollup, work/verification summaries | Priorities and consequence categories retain domain and currency; no opaque score. |
frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp.md:80:| Site Overview `GET /sites/{id}/overview` | Site objective cards, bounded metrics, timeline, latest Finding/consequence/action, source freshness | Mini-grid service/dispatch; cold temperature/backup; EV sessions/availability. |
frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp.md-81-| Performance `GET /sites/{id}/performance/{view}` | Windowed analytic revisions plus evidence coverage | Dispatch/service/fuel; temperature/cooling/backup; sessions/availability/energy. |
frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp.md:82:| Findings `GET /findings`, `/findings/{id}` | Stable Finding + revisions, alternatives, consequence and work links | Domain-specific mechanism, severity and claim limits. |
frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp.md-83-| Evidence `GET /sites/{id}/evidence`, `/evidence/{id}` | Capability matrix, accepted/raw/normalized records, source health, lineage, rejections | Role coverage required for each domain claim. |
frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp.md-84-| Financials `GET /sites/{id}/financials`, `/financials` | Consequence revisions, assumption versions and compatible rollups | Fuel/maintenance, product/reliability exposure, EV revenue/service loss; show unpriced risk. |
frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp.md-85-| Assets `GET /assets`, `/assets/{id}` | Asset identity, condition/stress evidence, warranty and linked work | Battery life, compressor/backup health, charger/connector condition. |
frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp.md-86-| Actions `GET /actions`, `/actions/{id}` | Plan, events, later-window guardrails, verification outcome | Plan target and guardrails follow Finding's domain. |
frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp.md-87-
frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp.md:88:These are API families, not a command to build every mock screen immediately. Site-scoped lists filter the **same** global Finding/Action objects. Routes without a supported read model stay out of navigation; supported routes with missing evidence show a reason and next acquisition step. Example claim path: generator state/power and PV/battery/load envelopes → accepted records tied to mapping/Foundation revisions → versioned dispatch interval classification → Finding revision + bounded fuel/maintenance consequence under assumption revision → Site/Portfolio projection → Finding card and evidence drawer. The drawer resolves exact IDs and original envelope bytes; it never reconstructs lineage from a card label.

frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp.md-104-| Disposition | Major pieces and rationale |
frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp.md-105-| --- | --- |
frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp.md-106-| **KEEP** | `contracts/assetops_contracts/source_envelope.py` strict parsing and provenance shape; `simulator/assetops_simulator/kernel/` causal world and `observation/` separation; `frontend/src/ui/` primitives, operator/Lab shell split, evidence inspection and useful Site/dispatch presentation. They protect the real/simulated boundary or already express the intended journey. |
frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp.md:107:| **ADAPT** | `simulator/.../gateway/` mapping/publication into a durable SQLite gateway with retry; `contracts/.../ingestion_batch.py` and `backend/.../evidence/service.py` into authenticated, transactional Postgres acceptance and per-record receipts; `backend/.../analytics/dispatch.py` into role-bound, windowed domain analytics; Site Foundation into time-valid configuration; `frontend/src/sites/` into domain-shaped read views. Preserve behavior through equivalence tests, not storage formats. |
frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp.md-108-| **REPLACE** | JSON whole-site evidence (`backend/.../evidence/adapters/json_evidence_store.py`), JSON assessment store, YAML mutable Site store and process-local write lock with Postgres repositories. Whole-file rewrites and local locks cannot provide concurrent multi-site product semantics. Keep Lab run inputs/artifacts private, moving large immutable run bytes to artifact storage as needed. Replace direct Lab in-process Commit-to-ingest call with the same authenticated batch boundary used by real gateways. |
frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp.md-109-| **RETIRE** | Hard-coded demonstration device keys in `backend/.../analytics/dispatch.py`; view calculations that independently reinterpret evidence; treating `source_mode` as sufficient authentication; old T0xx sequence as the implementation plan. These encode a single fixture or a demo deployment assumption. Keep historical fixtures/tests as migration evidence, not product authority. |
frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp.md-110-

frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp.md-112-
frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp.md-113-## Fresh vertical-slice sequence
frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp.md-114-
frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp.md:115:1. **One evidence path, two sources.** Version/fixture the envelope and batch receipt; build SQLite gateway outbox and authenticated HTTPS/Postgres ingestion. S2 proves the path with simulated input only. The first real pilot supplies one real capture or labelled vendor-format wire fixture to prove the physical adapter input and equivalent downstream behavior. Demo: unplug/reconnect, retries, deduplication, late arrival, rejection and inspectable Evidence lineage.
frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp.md:116:2. **Mini-grid decision with money.** Migrate Site/Foundation revisions and dispatch analytic to role-bound accepted evidence; produce windowed Finding and bounded fuel/maintenance consequence with editable, versioned assumptions. Demo: Portfolio → Site → dispatch Finding → raw evidence and exact money basis.
frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp.md-117-3. **Action and verification.** Persist recommendation/Action, plan revision, activity and later-window outcome with service/reserve guardrails. Demo: change a policy in Lab, commit later evidence, show Verified/Ineffective/Inconclusive and supported portfolio change.
frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp.md:118:4. **Cold-chain equivalence.** Add temperature/backup device roles and a bounded excursion/backup analytic, relevant asset and consequence view. Use the same gateway/API/shell/workflow. Demo: cold room source loss versus real excursion, with honest product-exposure wording.
frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp.md-119-5. **EV equivalence and estate view.** Add session/connector roles, interruption/availability analytic, revenue assumption and EV asset view; finish comparable Portfolio prioritization across three domains. Demo: one mini-grid, one cold-chain and one EV site, each traceable from source to action.
frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp.md-120-
frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp.md-121-Each slice ends with a screen-visible claim and an evidence-lineage check. Real-device drivers may start with one pilot protocol; broader protocol libraries, MQTT, stream infrastructure, predictive maintenance, extensive financial scenarios and generic domain plugins wait for observed need. At the first real pilot, the acceptance gate is a real or labelled vendor-format gateway batch replacing the simulator batch while backend, analytics and UI tests remain unchanged. S2–S7 demo acceptance uses simulated input and makes no physical protocol proof claim.

frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp-technical-design.md-32-
frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp-technical-design.md-33-| Table | Purpose; PK; columns; example row | FKs, unique constraints and indexes | Mutation / time |
frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp-technical-design.md-34-| --- | --- | --- | --- |
frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp-technical-design.md:35:| `ingestion_batch` | One immutable upload identity and durable receipt; `id`; `tenant_id`, `site_id`, `gateway_id`, `source_id`, `external_batch_id`, `payload_sha256`, `schema_version`, `foundation_revision_id`, `binding_set_id/version`, `status`, `total/processed/accepted/duplicate/rejected/conflict_count`, `received_at`, `completed_at`; `ib1 / s1 / batch-007 / sha256:… / COMPLETED / 12` | FK site/gateway/source/foundation same site; `UNIQUE(site_id,external_batch_id)`, `UNIQUE(tenant_id,site_id,id)`; index `(site_id,received_at DESC)` | Payload digest, ordering and reference immutable; counters/status advance transactionally. Same ID/digest returns prior receipt; same ID/different digest yields batch conflict, without changing prior receipt. |
frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp-technical-design.md-36-| `accepted_evidence` | One accepted public source fact; `id`; `tenant_id`, `site_id`, `batch_id`, `gateway_id`, `source_id`, `message_id`, `sequence_epoch`, `sequence`, `device_id`, `signal_id`, `asset_id`, `family`, `source_time`, `published_at`, `received_at`, `interval_start/end`, `quality`, `original_value_text/unit`, `normalized_value_text/unit`, `typed_value jsonb`, `envelope_sha256`, `envelope_text`, `foundation_revision_id`, `binding_revision_id`, `source_mode`; `e1 / s1 / msg:… / 10:02 / generator_state=RUNNING` | FK site/batch/gateway/source/foundation/binding same site; `UNIQUE(site_id,gateway_id,source_id,message_id)`; indexes `(site_id,source_time DESC)`, `(site_id,device_id,signal_id,source_time)`, `(site_id,binding_revision_id,source_time)`, `(batch_id)` | Append-only; never update a normalized value or replace a conflicting envelope. Retain canonical text initially in Postgres; move large raw bytes to immutable object storage later with hash and locator. |
frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp-technical-design.md-37-| `evidence_rejection` | Inspectable per-envelope rejection or ID conflict; `id`; `tenant_id`, `site_id`, `batch_id`, `ordinal`, `message_id?`, `kind`, `field`, `detail`, `envelope_sha256`, `conflicts_with_evidence_id?`, `received_at`; `r1 / ib1 / 4 / UNIT_INCOMPATIBLE / e1?` | FK site/batch, optional accepted evidence same site; `UNIQUE(batch_id,ordinal,kind,envelope_sha256)`; indexes `(site_id,received_at DESC)`, `(batch_id,ordinal)` | Append-only. Duplicate retry of a rejected entry returns same disposition, not a new rejection. Whole-batch refusals use `ordinal=NULL` and a distinct `(site_id,external_batch_id,digest)` identity in a batch-refusal ledger if no batch was accepted. |
frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp-technical-design.md:38:| `ingestion_disposition` | Exact per-ordinal receipt for retries, including duplicates; PK `(batch_id,ordinal)`; `message_id?`, `decision`, `accepted_evidence_id?`, `rejection_id?`, `decided_at`; `ib1 / 3 / DUPLICATE / e1` | FK batch, optional evidence/rejection same site; index `(batch_id,decision)` | Append-only; in same transaction as evidence decision and batch watermark. Decisions: `ACCEPTED,DUPLICATE,REJECTED,CONFLICT`. |
frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp-technical-design.md-39-| `recompute_job` | Transactional work queue; `id`; `tenant_id`, `site_id`, `domain`, `window_start/end`, `cause_evidence_id?`, `status`, `available_at`, `lease_until`, `attempts`; `j1 / s1 / dispatch / [10:00,11:00) / PENDING` | FK site/evidence; index `(status,available_at)` with pending filter; index `(site_id,window_start)` | Lease/status mutable. Coalesce overlapping dirty windows under site/domain lock; never lose the accepted record if worker fails. |
frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp-technical-design.md-40-
frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp-technical-design.md-41-For a complete batch refusal before parsing, store a `batch_refusal(id,tenant_id,site_id?,external_batch_id,payload_sha256,kind,detail,received_at)` row only when tenant/site are authenticated and resolvable; otherwise return a structured error without a Site ledger write. Its unique key is `(tenant_id,external_batch_id,payload_sha256)`. Authentication failures are security logs, not evidence. This keeps malformed batch attempts visible without pretending they were accepted batches.

frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp-technical-design.md-172-
frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp-technical-design.md:173:`QueryScope.window` is half-open `[start,end)` and required for analytic/product queries. `knowledge_at` makes historical audits reproducible; normal UI defaults to current server time. `selected_revision_id` freezes a drill-down even if the surrounding window changes. `AnalyticInputSet.input_sha256` hashes sorted evidence IDs **and their content digests**, resolved configuration/binding revisions, window, analytic/model version and canonical role coverage. The financial consequence has a separate basis digest including its assumption revisions. Missing required roles produce a bounded Unknown/Indeterminate assessment with explicit reason; the analytic never reads world state or a Lab run ID. `FinancialConsequence` has no amount when the technical quantity is supported but price/demand is not. `Measured`, `Modelled`, `Extrapolated` and `Scenario` are distinct bases; UI must expose them.
frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp-technical-design.md-174-
frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp-technical-design.md-175-## 4. Transaction and processing contracts
frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp-technical-design.md-176-
frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp-technical-design.md-177-1. **Register configuration.** Publish Foundation and SignalBinding versions together under Site lock. Validate site type, role vocabulary, units, asset ownership and non-overlapping current valid periods. Persist canonical snapshot hash for authenticated gateway download; no detached snapshot signature or key-rotation system is required in S1–S7. Backdated publication enqueues affected windows for recomputation; do not change accepted evidence bytes.
frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp-technical-design.md-178-2. **Receive batch.** TLS and gateway credential establish tenant/site/gateway/source. Enforce size/count limits before parsing; resolve external batch ID and digest. Identical retry returns stored receipt; conflicting retry records/refuses without touching prior decisions. A batch with wrong registered mode or configuration reference is refused as a whole.
frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp-technical-design.md-179-3. **Decide records.** In one Postgres transaction, lock batch row; for each unprocessed ordinal parse strict envelope, verify identity/sequence and referenced mapping/Foundation validity at `source_time`, normalize with the registered as-accepted conversion, and insert acceptance or rejection plus disposition. Unique message key arbitrates concurrent duplicate deliveries. Equal digest is `DUPLICATE`; changed digest is `CONFLICT` and points to first accepted evidence. Update watermark and create/coalesce recompute jobs in the same commit. Chunk a large batch into short committed transactions; receipt exposes `IN_PROGRESS` until all ordinals are durable. A client retries the same batch/bytes and resumes from the persisted watermark. Return a terminal receipt only after all decisions commit.
frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp-technical-design.md:180:4. **Assess.** Worker leases dirty site/domain/window jobs, selects accepted evidence by source time plus interval overlap at a declared knowledge cutoff, resolves the exact public configuration, builds `AnalyticInputSet`, and runs the versioned pure analytic. In one commit insert `AnalyticRun`, immutable assessment/finding/consequence revisions when basis changed, update projections and mark job done. Retry of same input hash is a no-op. Late or out-of-order evidence recomputes the affected and neighboring continuity window; it never mutates old revisions. Model deployment schedules explicit re-evaluation of supported windows.
frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp-technical-design.md-181-5. **Verify.** ActionEvent records work. After a later closed window exists, evaluate the frozen plan against accepted evidence, target and each guardrail. Missing coverage is `INCONCLUSIVE`; worsening service prevents `VERIFIED` even if cost falls. Link outcome to exact analytic run/evidence and surface any later superseding outcome.
frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp-technical-design.md-182-
frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp-technical-design.md:183:**Initial API contract:** `POST /api/ingestion/batches` returns terminal `200` receipt or `202 IN_PROGRESS` receipt; `GET /api/ingestion/batches/{id}` returns the same durable state. `409` is reserved for same batch ID/different bytes or registered identity conflict, `422` for malformed contract, `401/403` for authentication/authorization. Normal reads use `GET /api/sites/{id}/evidence`, `/evidence/records/{id}`, `/overview`, `/performance/{view}`, `/findings/{id}` with `from`, `to` and optional `revision_id`; list reads paginate by stable `(source_time,id)` or `(created_at,id)` cursor. All responses include scope/window, revision IDs, evidence coverage and projection watermark. The UI treats a lagging projection as pending, not healthy. Lab Commit calls this same HTTP boundary with a registered simulated gateway; no privileged product-write endpoint exists.
frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp-technical-design.md-184-
frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp-technical-design.md-185-**Gateway diagnostic API:** `POST /api/gateways/{gateway_id}/diagnostics` authenticates the registered gateway/site, checks `GatewayDiagnosticsV1` identity/hash and inserts `gateway_diagnostic` with server `received_at`; an identical retry returns the existing row. `409` refuses identity reuse with different bytes, `422` malformed fields, and `401/403` wrong credential/scope. `GET /api/sites/{id}/gateway-health` returns last server contact, declared cadence, source-contact observations, queue age/depth, disk pressure, clock offset and an explicit `CURRENT|STALE|UNKNOWN` status with diagnostic ID and receive time. It makes no claim that the device or asset itself is healthy. S2-01c publishes locally; S2-02 owns central storage and read API; S7 composes the Site Health claim.
frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp-technical-design.md-186-
frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp-technical-design.md:187:**Demo-to-Postgres cutover:** create Tenant/Site/Gateway/Source registrations from existing Site records, import immutable Foundation and mapping versions, then replay sealed committed batch bytes through the new ingestion service using the legacy parsers. Preserve original batch/message IDs, envelope text, source times and mode; assign new database UUIDs only to internal rows. Compare accepted/rejected/conflict counts and representative evidence/dispatch claims against the file-backed baseline. Keep `var/sites`, Lab runs, stages and committed artifacts untouched and read-only during verification. Switch product reads to Postgres only after the comparison passes; new Lab Commit posts the V2 batch/receipt path. Do not import private world trajectories into the product database. An existing batch whose required public configuration cannot be registered is reported as a migration exception, never guessed into a new Foundation version.
frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp-technical-design.md-188-
frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp-technical-design.md-189-**Acceptance checks for the first slices:** power loss after SQLite enqueue and after server commit; duplicate batch with identical bytes; same ID with different bytes; partial batch crash/retry; reordered and late samples; two same-timestamp samples with different sequences; mapping/Foundation version change during offline period; wrong gateway/site credential; one rejected sibling among valid samples; central diagnostic receipt/reload and stale-heartbeat behavior. S2–S7 use simulated input only. At the first real pilot, select one real capture or labelled vendor-format wire fixture and then compare physical and simulated normalized envelopes and downstream claims aside from provenance. Test lineage by opening a visible claim and resolving every evidence and assumption ID, rather than asserting only rendered text.
frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp-technical-design.md-190-

frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp-technical-design.md:202:| Financial scenarios | `financial_scenario` has stable Site/tenant-scoped scenario identity and owner; `financial_scenario_revision` freezes assumption revision IDs, window, parameters and formula version. | A new edit creates a revision; scenario runs/results are append-only, keyed by scenario revision and input digest. | Scenario consequence cites source Finding/quantity revision where used, and is always labelled `Scenario`. It cannot overwrite a measured/modelled operational consequence or be counted as recovered money. | Define allowed variables, cross-site scope, comparison baseline and currency/period rules before the Scenarios tab is live. |
frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp-technical-design.md-203-| Site onboarding | `site_draft` has its own UUID and tenant-scoped proposed code, separate from published `site.id`. `site_template` reuses the shipped catalog only as an explicitly versioned input; `site_import_batch` has stable upload identity and per-row disposition. | Draft revisions and setup events are append-only. Publish atomically allocates/resolves the Site code, initial Foundation and source registration; a failed batch row leaves no partial Site. Template updates never rewrite published Sites. | Persist source digest/template version and actor for every published Site; link import-row receipt to resulting Site/Foundation IDs. Imported physical/evidence claims still need gateway registration and accepted envelopes. | Define draft expiration, duplicate-code arbitration, template migration, batch atomicity/per-row retry and tenant authorization. The gated Lab create route is not the product onboarding contract. |
frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp-technical-design.md-204-
frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp-technical-design.md-205-**Shared projection rule.** `CapabilityReadiness`, `FindingIndex`, `SourceHealth`, `AssetHealthSummary` and later `MaintenanceIndex` have multiple screen consumers. Their first implementation must publish a backend read-model contract with scope/window, stable sort/pagination, freshness watermark, revision and drill-down IDs, Unknown/NotApplicable semantics and tenant isolation. A Site projection can feed a global root only after aggregation semantics are declared; shared names in the mock do not imply that the first Site query already serves the estate. `MaintenanceIndex` waits on maintenance identities above.
frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp-technical-design.md-206-
frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp-technical-design.md-207-**Root ownership rule before S4 and S6.** The Site Findings list is a Site-scoped projection; a Finding detail, Analysis, Evidence, Alternatives, Recommendation and History share stable Finding identity in the Findings root. S4's first detail route must preserve that identity and selected revision across tabs and links back to the Site. The Site Work list is a Site-scoped projection; Action Overview, Plan, Execution, Evidence, Verification and History share stable Action identity in the Actions root. Before S6 verification, define the Action-root route and plan/outcome revision URLs, preserving the Site/Finding back-links. Unimplemented tabs stay hidden; an empty tab must represent a real supported question.
frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp-technical-design.md-208-
frontend/docs/architecture/production-shaped-mvp-technical-design.md:209:**Demo data dependency.** One Kobo window cannot substantiate the multi-site, multi-window, cold-chain or EV mock claims. Before their packets, define a small seed manifest per Site/window with source mode, Foundation/binding versions, gateway fixture, accepted/rejected evidence, analytic version, assumptions and expected claim limits. Rehearse both a complete and an Unknown/contradictory case through normal ingestion; do not stage Findings or prices directly. Portfolio requires comparable periods, currency, coverage and overlap rules across the seeded Sites.

frontend/docs/architecture/site-first-execution-plan.md-80-
frontend/docs/architecture/site-first-execution-plan.md-81-## Shared implementation rules
frontend/docs/architecture/site-first-execution-plan.md-82-
frontend/docs/architecture/site-first-execution-plan.md:83:- **Immediate demo scope versus target design:** S1–S2 need crash-safe SQLite enqueue, stable identities, authenticated batch ingestion, durable per-record decisions and Postgres Site/evidence storage. Build the minimal diagnostics needed to inspect delivery. Detailed queue retention policy, elaborate backpressure controls, multi-protocol drivers, broker transport, full bitemporal editing UI and generic streaming infrastructure can follow when pilots require them. Database/wire schemas should leave room without falsely claiming those behaviors are shipped.
frontend/docs/architecture/site-first-execution-plan.md-84-- **Typed analytic boundary:** `EvidenceQuery` returns typed normalized readings/records with quality, time, asset/role, configuration basis and accepted-evidence IDs. The dispatch analytic consumes this plus a typed public configuration view. SQL rows, `jsonb`, canonical envelope text, Lab world records and source adapters stop at repository/normalization boundaries. Contract tests compare simulated and captured real inputs after this boundary.
frontend/docs/architecture/site-first-execution-plan.md-85-- **Episode identity:** decide continuity tolerance, episode anchor and recurrence linkage in S3/S4 domain design, with a short decision table before coding the first Finding. `Finding.id` names a durable operational episode; `FindingRevision` describes reassessment of that episode. A new separated episode is a new Finding, linked as recurrence if relevant. Do not use a changing model output hash as Finding identity.
frontend/docs/architecture/site-first-execution-plan.md-86-- **Domain algorithms:** dispatch thresholds, cold-chain excursion detection and EV session logic are domain-design work. The platform plan requires their inputs, revision/lineage outputs and claim limits, not more universal framework or algorithm prose. S1–S7 cover one mini-grid Site; later domains add role sets and analytics through the same seam.
--
frontend/docs/architecture/site-first-execution-plan.md-88-
frontend/docs/architecture/site-first-execution-plan.md-89-## Handoff beyond Site
frontend/docs/architecture/site-first-execution-plan.md-90-
frontend/docs/architecture/site-first-execution-plan.md:91:After S4, a **Findings index** can already list real Finding objects, but build the global root only when the Site detail and lifecycle are useful. After S6, **Actions** and **Sites** become filtering/indexing over durable Site/Action summaries; **Portfolio** aggregates comparable consequences and priorities with currency/window/coverage boundaries; **Evidence** aggregates Site capability/readiness; **Assets** indexes Site Asset/Health/work records after S7. These are later product roots, not prerequisites or empty screens for the Site spine. Cold-chain and EV are later Site-domain passes using the same ingestion, revision, work and shell contracts; neither should be built by copying mini-grid screens with different labels.

 succeeded in 2805ms:

`AUDIT_ONLY` with no amount. Alternative: HISTORICAL with reason `SOURCE_CLOSED`. A separate state keeps "earlier 
version" and "source not open" distinguishable in the copy.
  
> ## Q11. How the demonstration Finding is labelled
  Nothing in the evidence records which batch was authored for the demonstration (P01-power is posted with the 
registered gateway credential, like every S4 case). Built: `backend/assetops_backend/demonstration_label.py` reads the 
spec `conversions/s4-power-demo-cases-v1.json` and labels a Finding whose window lies wholly inside a declared case 
day at the declared Site (KOBO-001, 2026-10-23). It is a presentation label only; it changes no number or state. Risk: 
any later KOBO-001 Finding on 2026-10-23 would also be labelled. Better long-term: record authorship (a batch or 
source label) at ingestion; a Planner/Architect item, linked to the owner backlog "analysis input data for simulated 
and real Sites".

"""S4-02b phase 2b: label the authored demonstration Finding in product reads.

Owner ruling `.agent/owner-S4-02b-money-demo-2026-10-14.md`: the `P01-power` case of
`conversions/s4-power-demo-cases-v1.json` is authored demonstration content and must be visibly labelled
as such beside ordinary Findings. Nothing in the evidence rows records that authorship (the case is posted
with the registered gateway credential, like every S4 case), so the label is derived from the spec's own
declaration: its Site code and its case days. The spec's days are tested clear of every S4 case day.

This is a presentation label only. It never changes a quantity, price, amount or currentness.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import json
from datetime import date, datetime, timedelta, timezone
from functools import lru_cache
from pathlib import Path
from typing import Any

SPEC_PATH = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[1] / "conversions" / "s4-power-demo-cases-v1.json"

LABEL = "Demonstration data"
STATEMENT = ("This finding comes from demonstration readings written by the AssetOps team to show a complete "
             "fuel-cost calculation. They are not measurements from a working generator.")


UNVERIFIED_LABEL = "Source not confirmed"
UNVERIFIED_STATEMENT = ("Whether this finding comes from demonstration readings could not be checked, so treat any "
                        "cost shown here with caution.")


class SpecUnreadable(Exception):
    pass


@lru_cache(maxsize=1)
def _declared() -> tuple[str, str, tuple[tuple[str, date], ...]]:
    # Fails closed: an unreadable or malformed spec raises (and is therefore never cached), and the caller
    # then labels the Finding cautiously rather than leaving possible demonstration money unlabelled.
    try:
        spec = json.loads(SPEC_PATH.read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
        return (spec["site_code"], spec["label"],
                tuple((c["case_id"], date.fromisoformat(c["day"])) for c in spec["cases"]))
    except (OSError, ValueError, KeyError, TypeError) as exc:
        raise SpecUnreadable(str(exc)) from exc


def _instant(value: Any) -> datetime | None:
    if value is None:
        return None
    if isinstance(value, datetime):
        return value if value.tzinfo else value.replace(tzinfo=timezone.utc)
    try:
        parsed = datetime.fromisoformat(str(value).replace("Z", "+00:00"))
    except ValueError:
        return None
    return parsed if parsed.tzinfo else parsed.replace(tzinfo=timezone.utc)


def demonstration_case(site_code: str, start: Any, end: Any) -> dict[str, str] | None:
    """The declared demonstration case whose UTC day holds `[start, end)` at this Site, else None.

    Fails closed: an unreadable spec gives a cautious "source not confirmed" label, and an open episode
    (no end) is judged by its start day, so demonstration money is never shown unlabelled.
    """
    try:
        code, spec_label, cases = _declared()
    except SpecUnreadable:
        return {"label": UNVERIFIED_LABEL, "statement": UNVERIFIED_STATEMENT, "case_id": "unverified", "spec": ""}
    lo, hi = _instant(start), _instant(end)
    if site_code != code or lo is None:
        return None
    for case_id, day in cases:
        day_start = datetime(day.year, day.month, day.day, tzinfo=timezone.utc)
        if day_start <= lo and (hi is None or hi <= day_start + timedelta(days=1)) and lo < day_start + timedelta(days=1):
            return {"label": LABEL, "statement": STATEMENT, "case_id": case_id, "spec": spec_label}
    return None



 succeeded in 2821ms:

    - **Column updates on assignment rows.** UPDATEs of `id`, `revision_id` and `recorded_from` are refused by the
      immutability trigger. Reopening a closed `recorded_to` to NULL is refused ("already closed").
    - **Two Site series.** Two Sites each get `revision_no = 1`, with no interference between them.
    - **Re-pricing the scheduled v2 at its exact start (2027-01-01).**
>     - As a SUCCESSION: refused, `SUCCESSION_OVERLAP_NOT_PERMITTED`.
      - As an open-ended CORRECTION: refused, `CORRECTION_NOT_BACKDATED_OR_BOUNDED`.
      - A **bounded backdated full-window CORRECTION over an Oct-Dec 2026 window succeeds** (revision 3). So the S4-02b
        need (a backdated full-window correction over a Finding window) is not blocked.
    - **Fixture replay after a byte-level change.** Two cases were tested against a DB that already holds v1-v3:
      - appending a v4 step to the fixture;
      - flipping only the line endings.
  
      Both re-runs exit 3 at **v1** with `IDEMPOTENCY_KEY_REUSED`; see M1.
    - **Retroactive close.** An UPDATE that closes a current assignment at `recorded_from + 1Âµs`, which is earlier 
than
      later publications, is **accepted** by the database; see L1.
  
  ## Focus areas
  
  1. **Migration 0010.**
     - The EXCLUDE (`0010:103-106`) is character-for-character the packet's shape.
     - The non-empty CHECKs hold on both axes (`:100-101`), and on the revision too (`:72`).
     - The set-once trigger (`:121-139`):
       - compares `to_jsonb(NEW) - 'recorded_to'` with OLD;
     - The UPDATEs that close rows run before the INSERTs of new rows (`store:159-171`), so the non-deferrable 
exclusion
       is satisfied mid-transaction.
     - The downgrade is safe; see the checks above.
  2. **change_kind.**
>    - INITIAL, SUCCESSION and CORRECTION match packet item 2 and R1.
>    - **Deviation: SUCCESSION/CORRECTION over uncovered time is refused.** This is consistent with the packet, which
       defines uncovered time as INITIAL, and it blocks nothing S4-02b needs. A CORRECTION that partly covers unpriced
       time is still allowed.
     - **Deviation: an open-ended future CORRECTION is refused.** This is the packet's literal "backdated or bounded".
>    - **Unlisted deviation (L2).** A SUCCESSION exactly at the start of an open-ended assignment is refused. This
       matches the residual risk the plan re-review already recorded, and S4-02b does not need it.
  3. **Idempotency and concurrency.** All five points hold:
     - The lock is taken first (`store:108-110`), keyed by tenant, Site and key, and it is transaction-scoped.
     - The replay is checked before the prior check, so a retry after success replays rather than raising 409.
     - A different digest returns 409.
     - A stale prior returns 409.
     - `clock_timestamp()` is read after the lock.
  
     Both race tests are real; see the probe.
  4. **Actor authority.**
     - `SYSTEM_PUBLISHER` is a code constant, and the CLI has no actor or author flag.
     - The store refuses a non-SYSTEM principal.
     - The DB CHECK ties HUMAN to `author_id` and to `HUMAN_API`.
     - The route refuses in the order 401, then 422, then 403/404, then a 401 for an admitted request
       (`financial_api.py:61-87`).
     - It does not import `development_access`, and that is tested.
  5. **Guard.**
     - The rule-8 writer list and per-table ownership are added symmetrically to the existing Finding rule.
  - the test sets `soon = now + 20 days` (`test_s4_assumptions_postgres.py:190`).
  
  Failure scenarios:

    fails with DID NOT RAISE.
> - **From 2027-01-01.** Every fixture run on a fresh DB is refused at v2 with `SUCCESSION_NOT_FUTURE`. That breaks the
    restart test, the history-rewrite test and the walkthrough recipe.
  
  This lands inside the expected S4-02b and owner-walkthrough period, which is 60-80 days away.
  
  Fix (before the walkthrough DB is published, because of M1):
  - move v2 well into the future, for example `2030-01-01`;
> - derive the test's SUCCESSION instant from the v2 date (for example `v2 - 30 days`, asserted to be later than now)
    instead of `now + 20 days`.
  
  ### Low
  
  **L1. The set-once trigger allows a retroactive close.** (`0010:121-139`)
  
  `recorded_to` may be set to any instant later than `recorded_from`, including one earlier than later publications in
  the series.
  
  Failure scenario: a direct SQL writer closes a current row at `recorded_from + 1Âµs`, which the probe showed is
  accepted. That silently changes what earlier recorded cutoffs select, and can leave an unpriced hole at a cutoff.
  - Only the guarded store writes these tables, and it always closes at a fresh `clock_timestamp()` later than the
    series' latest `recorded_from`. So this is defence in depth, not a live bug.
  - The `foundation_revision` precedent has the same gap.
  
  Fix (optional; cheapest before merge, otherwise it needs a new migration): in the immutability trigger, when OLD
  `recorded_to` is NULL and NEW is not, require NEW `recorded_to` > the series' `max(recorded_from)`.
  
> **L2. Unlisted deviation: a SUCCESSION at exactly the start of the open-ended assignment is refused.**
  (`assumptions.py:196`, `not only.valid_from < valid_from`)
  
> The packet says SUCCESSION "may only truncate the single open-ended current assignment **containing** `valid_from`".
  Half-open `[2027-01-01, âˆž)` contains 2027-01-01. The strict `<` refuses that case, and an open-ended future 
CORRECTION
  is also refused, so re-pricing an already-scheduled future price from its own start has no open-ended form. Only a
  bounded CORRECTION with an arbitrary far end can do it.
  
  This is pure plan code, with no schema impact and nothing S4-02b needs. The plan re-review already listed the related
  residual risk.
  
  Fix: record it as a listed deviation in the phase 2 handoff, and send it to the Architect with the next batched
  questions. Do not change it now.
  
  ### Nit
  
  - **Guard granularity.** The rule-8 regexes are per line. A single source line holding two statements, the second of
    them an arbitrary UPDATE, would pass. The DB triggers backstop this, and the same is true of the other writers.
  - **Route body.** `financial_api.py:66` reads an unbounded request body before the 422 check. This is acceptable 
while
    the route is 401 by default, but worth a size cap when a human path exists.

  **Ruling needed:** whether the UI should merge `STALE` and `PENDING` into the packet's single "STALE/PENDING" label. 
Phase 2b can map them either way.
  
> ## Q2. Currentness is a fresh derivation at read time, not a price-only comparison
  The read runs `load_input` + `quantify` + the window price selection for the selected latest FindingRevision, then 
compares the full consequence basis with the head's. After M1, that basis holds the selected price revision, or the 
window-clipped price boundaries, plus the technical digest.
  
  Price-only comparison would miss one case. A late-accepted power reading inside the window changes the technical 
input, yet no work row is enqueued for evidence acceptance (0011 triggers fire only on price publication and 
FindingRevision insert). The head is then shown `STALE (TECHNICAL_INPUT_CHANGED)`, never as current, until a recompute 
runs.
  
  **Ruling needed:** whether evidence acceptance should also enqueue consequence work. That would be a new trigger on 
`accepted_evidence`, outside this phase.
  
  ## Q3. Replay of a non-head basis (phase-1 Q6)
  An unchanged basis replays the stored ID. If an exact earlier state ever recurred (A, then B, then A), `append` 
would return revision 1 while the head is revision 2. The read would then report `STALE` until a different state 
appends.
  
  This is fail-closed, and in practice unreachable:
  - price revisions and FindingRevisions are append-only with fresh IDs;
  - the M1 basis carries revision IDs, so a "return" to an earlier price is always a new revision ID.
  
  ## Q4. Pre-0011 data (review L4): both options taken
  - **Bootstrap.** Migration 0011 enqueues one `FINDING_REVISION_APPENDED` work row for every FindingRevision that 
exists when it runs. This is the same row the trigger would have written. The test downgrades to 0010 and re-upgrades 
with Findings present.
  - **Defence in depth.** The read reports "no consequence" as `PENDING`, never as absent or zero. If no work row 
exists either, the reason is `NOT_YET_COMPUTED_NO_WORK_RECORDED`.
  
  Price revisions are not backfilled. The Finding work prices with the current timeline, which already includes them.
  
  ## Q5. Work targets
  - `FINDING_REVISION_APPENDED` recomputes that exact revision, even if a later one has superseded it. Per the packet, 
"a new FindingRevision produces its own linked consequence."
  - `ASSUMPTION_PUBLISHED` recomputes only each overlapping Finding's **latest** revision. Superseded revisions keep 
their consequences as history and are not repriced.
  
  ## Q6. The worker is a one-shot CLI, not a daemon
  There is no in-process scheduler:
      a CURRENT head's amount is by construction the amount the inputs give now.
    - The read takes the chain snapshot and the derivation in separate transactions. Every interleaving I could 
construct
      produces a false STALE, never a false CURRENT, except one microsecond window. In that window a technical input
>     changes between `load_input` and the price snapshot, and no work row exists for that change anyway (Q2).
    - HISTORICAL never derives and never presents as current: verified by probe, but untested (M2).
  - **Rounding** (`consequence_read.py:49-66`):
    - The low bound uses `ROUND_FLOOR` and the high bound `ROUND_CEILING`, to the ISO minor unit.
    - The 33-entry table is correct for the 0- and 3-digit currencies listed.
    - An unknown currency gives no display amount, and the bridge shows none.
    - `quantity.high_display` is ceilinged to 0.01 L.
  - **Route** (`finding_api.py:245-277`):
    - Scope and access come first.
    - A store that is absent gives 503 (tested). A store that fails also gives 503, through `consequence_down`; this is
      untested at route level, but the bridge degrades to `UNAVAILABLE` (tested).

+# Architect rulings: S4-02 batch (2026-10-14)
+
+Authority: `D-2026-10-13-s4-bounded-financial-basis`, the S4-02a/b packets and fresh reviews. S4-02a/b and the conditional owner checkpoint remain accepted; these rulings identify follow-on work and the precise registry boundary.
+
+## Q1 — SUCCESSION at the current assignment's start
+
+**Permit it.** The packet says the single open-ended assignment containing `valid_from` may be succeeded; a half-open assignment contains its start. Require `valid_from >= recorded_at`, the expected prior revision, one overlapping open-ended assignment, and the existing authorization/idempotency checks. At equality, close the old assignment's recorded-time tenure and insert only the new assignment for `[valid_from, ∞)`; do not insert an empty left remainder. The old price and its former scheduled assignment remain available at earlier recorded cutoffs. This is an audited replacement of a future schedule, not a rewrite or a backdated correction. More than one overlap, a bounded assignment or a stale expected prior still returns 409.
+
+**Code:** yes, a small S4-02 follow-on before relying on future schedule replacement: update the publication planner and tests for equal-start success, cutoff history, retry and concurrent prior mismatch. No owner choice is needed; this enforces the packet's stated boundary.
+
+## Q2 — late accepted evidence and consequence work
+
+**Enqueue on acceptance.** Finding re-evaluation alone is insufficient: the current read already detects a changed technical digest for the *same* FindingRevision after a late accepted power interval, while migration 0011 records work only for price publication and FindingRevision insertion. A `STALE (TECHNICAL_INPUT_CHANGED)` read is an honest safety net, not a complete recompute workflow. Record a durable, retryable work item transactionally with accepted evidence for potentially affected Site/window Findings; the worker must recheck exact role/asset/window and the latest eligible FindingRevision before deriving, so unrelated evidence can no-op. Preserve the existing FindingRevision-insert trigger for genuinely revised Findings. Deduplicate retries by accepted evidence identity and target/cause, and never append an identical consequence basis twice. The UI may say work is queued only when such a row exists; a one-shot worker still needs an explicit run/scheduling plan.
+
+**Code:** yes, an S4-02 consequence follow-on, before S5 Overview consumes current money. It owns the migration/work selector, acceptance integration, worker idempotency and a late-power acceptance → pending/stale → recompute → new version walkthrough. Coordinate with S2 ingestion and S4 Finding re-evaluation, but do not make those slices the sole trigger. No new owner decision is needed.
+
+## Q3 — demonstration provenance
+
+**Store provenance at ingestion.** A Site/day match to an authored spec is not proof that a particular accepted reading was authored for demonstration. The current label is acceptable for the reviewed, isolated S4-02 demonstration and its fail-closed `Source not confirmed` behavior, but it can falsely label a real KOBO-001 Finding on 2026-10-23 and is not tenant scoped. Add an immutable, tenant/Site-scoped, server-authorized batch/publication source designation with fixture/spec version and publication identity; bind it to accepted evidence IDs and carry the cited evidence's designation into Finding/consequence reads. Gateway credentials or client-declared text alone must not confer authorship. Mixed or unresolvable inputs must show a cautious source state, not claim that the whole Finding is demonstration data or real measurements. Retire the date heuristic once this path is live.
+
+**Code:** yes, a source-provenance follow-on spanning S2 ingestion and S4 reads/UI. It must land **before any real-Site pilot and before W1 exit**; until then the current demonstration label is evidence only for the bounded S4-02 fixture, not a general source-authorship capability. **Owner input:** ask the owner to approve the visible wording for mixed/unknown provenance and the pilot data designation at the W1 checkpoint. The need for stored provenance is an architecture requirement, not conditional on that copy choice.
+
+## Q4 — `assumptions:3#a` and consequence history
+
+**Per-Finding versions do not satisfy the registered claim.** `assumptions:3#a` says *prior assumption and consequence revisions at selected cutoff* on the Assumptions interpretation-history surface. The built Assumptions view selects price history at a recorded cutoff; Overview lists consequence versions only for one selected FindingRevision, with exact price links and no consequence cutoff selection. The latter is valid `I-consequence-history-read` evidence for a per-Finding read, but it does not answer which Site consequences used a price or what the Site knew at the chosen cutoff. Exact-ID links alone do not provide that query.
+
+**Code:** yes, an S4-02 Assumptions-history follow-on before `assumptions:3#a` promotion and W1 exit. Add a tenant/Site-scoped, paged consequence-history read at the selected recorded cutoff, with exact Finding/FindingRevision/consequence and price revision IDs, recorded/valid times, historical/current-at-cutoff distinction, and typed missing/closed-source states. The Assumptions view must expose those versions beside or from the selected price history, with an exact link back to the Finding; no fabricated estate total is implied. Review cutoff boundaries, pagination, foreign-Site refusal and app/mock states.
+
+**Registry closeout now:** after recording the accepted S4-02b reviews and owner checkpoint, the coordinator may resolve the supported S4-02 storage, financial-basis, per-Finding read/history and UI nodes and `I-S4-02b`/`I-S4-02`; promote `financials:1#a`, `financials:2#a` and `assumptions:1#a` with their actual evidence. Record the price-history subclaim of `assumptions:3#a` as built evidence, but leave the **whole core portion `assumptions:3#a` open** and its panel partial. Do not relabel that core portion as delivered or move its missing consequence-at-cutoff obligation into the non-core G-05 bucket. G-05 may still track the separate non-core Assumptions and Financials mock omissions. No owner decision is needed to preserve the registered claim; the owner should see the partial W1 status.
+
+## Draft DECISIONS entry
+
+Addendum to `D-2026-10-13-s4-bounded-financial-basis` (S4-02 post-review): A future `SUCCESSION` may replace the sole open-ended current Site price assignment at its exact `valid_from`, provided the prior revision, authorization and idempotency checks pass; preserve the previous recorded-time history and create no empty assignment. Accepted evidence that may change an exact FindingRevision's fuel technical basis must durably enqueue scoped, idempotent consequence work; a fresh `STALE` read does not replace that work. Demonstration authorship must be recorded by a server-authorized, tenant/Site-scoped ingestion publication and traced through accepted evidence before a real-Site pilot or W1 exit; Site/day matching is only a bounded fixture label. `assumptions:3#a` requires Site consequence history selectable at the same recorded cutoff as assumption history; per-Finding Overview versions alone do not close it. Existing reviewed S4-02 money claims may close independently, while this core history portion stays open until its read and UI are reviewed.
+
+## Owner-facing summary
+
+The current fuel-cost screen can close its reviewed money claims. Four follow-ons remain: allow an already-scheduled future price to be replaced at its start; queue recalculation when late readings arrive; record which readings were published as demonstration data; and show Site fuel-cost history at the chosen Assumptions cutoff. The last two must be in place before W1 exit, and recorded source provenance must also precede any real-Site pilot. Until the Site history is built, Assumptions history is only partially delivered. The owner should settle the wording for mixed or unknown data sources at the W1 checkpoint.

+# Architect rulings: S4-02 batch (2026-10-14)
+
+Authority: `D-2026-10-13-s4-bounded-financial-basis`, the S4-02a/b packets and fresh reviews. S4-02a/b and the conditional owner checkpoint remain accepted; these rulings identify follow-on work and the precise registry boundary.
+
+## Q1 — SUCCESSION at the current assignment's start
+
+**Permit it.** The packet says the single open-ended assignment containing `valid_from` may be succeeded; a half-open assignment contains its start. Require `valid_from >= recorded_at`, the expected prior revision, one overlapping open-ended assignment, and the existing authorization/idempotency checks. At equality, close the old assignment's recorded-time tenure and insert only the new assignment for `[valid_from, ∞)`; do not insert an empty left remainder. The old price and its former scheduled assignment remain available at earlier recorded cutoffs. This is an audited replacement of a future schedule, not a rewrite or a backdated correction. More than one overlap, a bounded assignment or a stale expected prior still returns 409.

Accepted evidence that may change an exact FindingRevision's fuel technical basis must durably enqueue scoped, idempotent consequence work; a fresh `STALE` read does not replace that work. Demonstration authorship must be recorded by a server-authorized, tenant/Site-scoped ingestion publication and traced through accepted evidence before a real-Site pilot or W1 exit; Site/day matching is only a bounded fixture label. `assumptions:3#a` requires Site consequence history selectable at the same recorded cutoff as assumption history; per-Finding Overview versions alone do not close it. Existing reviewed S4-02 money claims may close independently, while this core history portion stays open until its read and UI are reviewed.
+
+## Owner-facing summary
+
+The current fuel-cost screen can close its reviewed money claims. Four follow-ons remain: allow an already-scheduled future price to be replaced at its start; queue recalculation when late readings arrive; record which readings were published as demonstration data; and show Site fuel-cost history at the chosen Assumptions cutoff. The last two must be in place before W1 exit, and recorded source provenance must also precede any real-Site pilot. Until the Site history is built, Assumptions history is only partially delivered. The owner should settle the wording for mixed or unknown data sources at the W1 checkpoint.

+# Architect rulings: S4-02 batch (2026-10-14)
+
+Authority: `D-2026-10-13-s4-bounded-financial-basis`, the S4-02a/b packets and fresh reviews. S4-02a/b and the conditional owner checkpoint remain accepted; these rulings identify follow-on work and the precise registry boundary.
+
+## Q1 — SUCCESSION at the current assignment's start
+
+**Permit it.** The packet says the single open-ended assignment containing `valid_from` may be succeeded; a half-open assignment contains its start. Require `valid_from >= recorded_at`, the expected prior revision, one overlapping open-ended assignment, and the existing authorization/idempotency checks. At equality, close the old assignment's recorded-time tenure and insert only the new assignment for `[valid_from, ∞)`; do not insert an empty left remainder. The old price and its former scheduled assignment remain available at earlier recorded cutoffs. This is an audited replacement of a future schedule, not a rewrite or a backdated correction. More than one overlap, a bounded assignment or a stale expected prior still returns 409.
+
+**Code:** yes, a small S4-02 follow-on before relying on future schedule replacement: update the publication planner and tests for equal-start success, cutoff history, retry and concurrent prior mismatch. No owner choice is needed; this enforces the packet's stated boundary.
+
+## Q2 — late accepted evidence and consequence work
+
+**Enqueue on acceptance.** Finding re-evaluation alone is insufficient: the current read already detects a changed technical digest for the *same* FindingRevision after a late accepted power interval, while migration 0011 records work only for price publication and FindingRevision insertion. A `STALE (TECHNICAL_INPUT_CHANGED)` read is an honest safety net, not a complete recompute workflow. Record a durable, retryable work item transactionally with accepted evidence for potentially affected Site/window Findings; the worker must recheck exact role/asset/window and the latest eligible FindingRevision before deriving, so unrelated evidence can no-op. Preserve the existing FindingRevision-insert trigger for genuinely revised Findings. Deduplicate retries by accepted evidence identity and target/cause, and never append an identical consequence basis twice. The UI may say work is queued only when such a row exists; a one-shot worker still needs an explicit run/scheduling plan.
+
+**Code:** yes, an S4-02 consequence follow-on, before S5 Overview consumes current money. It owns the migration/work selector, acceptance integration, worker idempotency and a late-power acceptance → pending/stale → recompute → new version walkthrough. Coordinate with S2 ingestion and S4 Finding re-evaluation, but do not make those slices the sole trigger. No new owner decision is needed.
+
+## Q3 — demonstration provenance
+
+**Store provenance at ingestion.** A Site/day match to an authored spec is not proof that a particular accepted reading was authored for demonstration. The current label is acceptable for the reviewed, isolated S4-02 demonstration and its fail-closed `Source not confirmed` behavior, but it can falsely label a real KOBO-001 Finding on 2026-10-23 and is not tenant scoped. Add an immutable, tenant/Site-scoped, server-authorized batch/publication source designation with fixture/spec version and publication identity; bind it to accepted evidence IDs and carry the cited evidence's designation into Finding/consequence reads. Gateway credentials or client-declared text alone must not confer authorship. Mixed or unresolvable inputs must show a cautious source state, not claim that the whole Finding is demonstration data or real measurements. Retire the date heuristic once this path is live.
+
+**Code:** yes, a source-provenance follow-on spanning S2 ingestion and S4 reads/UI. It must land **before any real-Site pilot and before W1 exit**; until then the current demonstration label is evidence only for the bounded S4-02 fixture, not a general source-authorship capability. **Owner input:** ask the owner to approve the visible wording for mixed/unknown provenance and the pilot data designation at the W1 checkpoint. The need for stored provenance is an architecture requirement, not conditional on that copy choice.
+
+## Q4 — `assumptions:3#a` and consequence history
+
+**Per-Finding versions do not satisfy the registered claim.** `assumptions:3#a` says *prior assumption and consequence revisions at selected cutoff* on the Assumptions interpretation-history surface. The built Assumptions view selects price history at a recorded cutoff; Overview lists consequence versions only for one selected FindingRevision, with exact price links and no consequence cutoff selection. The latter is valid `I-consequence-history-read` evidence for a per-Finding read, but it does not answer which Site consequences used a price or what the Site knew at the chosen cutoff. Exact-ID links alone do not provide that query.
+
+**Code:** yes, an S4-02 Assumptions-history follow-on before `assumptions:3#a` promotion and W1 exit. Add a tenant/Site-scoped, paged consequence-history read at the selected recorded cutoff, with exact Finding/FindingRevision/consequence and price revision IDs, recorded/valid times, historical/current-at-cutoff distinction, and typed missing/closed-source states. The Assumptions view must expose those versions beside or from the selected price history, with an exact link back to the Finding; no fabricated estate total is implied. Review cutoff boundaries, pagination, foreign-Site refusal and app/mock states.
+
+**Registry closeout now:** after recording the accepted S4-02b reviews and owner checkpoint, the coordinator may resolve the supported S4-02 storage, financial-basis, per-Finding read/history and UI nodes and `I-S4-02b`; `I-S4-02` may resolve as the reviewed a/b task only if its acceptance text is corrected so it no longer promises promotion of four supported W1 portions. Promote `financials:1#a`, `financials:2#a` and `assumptions:1#a` with their actual evidence. Record the price-history subclaim of `assumptions:3#a` as built evidence, but leave the **whole core portion `assumptions:3#a` open** and its panel partial. Do not relabel that core portion as delivered or move its missing consequence-at-cutoff obligation into the non-core G-05 bucket. G-05 may still track the separate non-core Assumptions and Financials mock omissions. No owner decision is needed to preserve the registered claim; the owner should see the partial W1 status.
+
+## Draft DECISIONS entry
+
+Addendum to `D-2026-10-13-s4-bounded-financial-basis` (S4-02 post-review): A future `SUCCESSION` may replace the sole open-ended current Site price assignment at its exact `valid_from`, provided the prior revision, authorization and idempotency checks pass; preserve the previous recorded-time history and create no empty assignment. Accepted evidence that may change an exact FindingRevision's fuel technical basis must durably enqueue scoped, idempotent consequence work; a fresh `STALE` read does not replace that work. Demonstration authorship must be recorded by a server-authorized, tenant/Site-scoped ingestion publication and traced through accepted evidence before a real-Site pilot or W1 exit; Site/day matching is only a bounded fixture label. `assumptions:3#a` requires Site consequence history selectable at the same recorded cutoff as assumption history; per-Finding Overview versions alone do not close it. Existing reviewed S4-02 money claims may close independently, while this core history portion stays open until its read and UI are reviewed.
+
+## Owner-facing summary
+
+The current fuel-cost screen can close its reviewed money claims. Four follow-ons remain: allow an already-scheduled future price to be replaced at its start; queue recalculation when late readings arrive; record which readings were published as demonstration data; and show Site fuel-cost history at the chosen Assumptions cutoff. The last two must be in place before W1 exit, and recorded source provenance must also precede any real-Site pilot. Until the Site history is built, Assumptions history is only partially delivered. The owner should settle the wording for mixed or unknown data sources at the W1 checkpoint.
